# The build log

The build session's log: one line per step, newest last, and a STOP-REPORT when the
builder stops. The guard and the Stop hook append their own lines here (a blocked
settings change, "STOPPED RED"). The builder may write this file (R-79); nobody edits
a line once it is committed.

## How to stop: the STOP-REPORT

A STOP-REPORT is the builder's legal exit when a test, the spec or a rule seems wrong
(R-83). A turn can't end while `engine/tools/check fast` is red, unless a STOP-REPORT
heading was added to this file since the last commit, or by it. A rule change starts
here and goes through R-112's procedure: the lead reproduces it, the user approves it
in plain words, dated in the rulebook, and the `rules` role commits it.

Copy the template below to the end of this file, without the four-space indent (the
indent keeps the template itself from counting as a report):

    ## STOP-REPORT 2026-MM-DD: <one line: what blocks the build>

    - **Kind:** a test seems wrong | the spec seems wrong | a rule blocks correct code | other
    - **Where:** <file:line of the code, the test, the vector or the spec line>
    - **Rule or gate:** <R-n, or DONE gate n, or the spec's section>
    - **What happens:** <the command, quoted, with its exit code and the first error line>
    - **What I tried:** <the strict alternative tried, and why it doesn't work>
    - **What I ask:** <the change asked for, or the question for the lead>
    - **State:** <the last green commit; what is uncommitted>

## 2026-11-01 S, G1: numbers (issue #26)

**Built.** `crates/tally/src/num/` (kernel): `rational.rs` (`Num`: a reduced fraction, small `i64`/`NonZeroU64` or big, 16 bytes; demotion after every big result; the 4 096-digit cap by bit length, 10^4096 compared only at 13 607 bits), `small.rs` (128-bit intermediates, the whole-number shortcut), `big.rs` (the one big-integer file: `Big`, `BigRat`), `gcd.rs` (Euclid on words; Lehmer, Knuth 4.5.2 Algorithm L, with Collins's test; big by small), `round.rs` (the seven modes of L §7.2 at any positive step), `money.rs` (`Money` in minor units, `Word | Big`; currencies never mix), `text.rs` (literals read exactly; printing as refcore's `values.py`: groups of three, the shortest exact decimal, the sig15 aid). `lib.rs` names the files in an inline `pub mod num { … }`, since `mod.rs` is denied (`mod_module_files`) and the code stays under the kernel's `num/**`.

**Green.** No T id: G1 has none of its own; it feeds every money line, P3 and P8-M1..M3. The builder's 33 unit tests (dev and release), with Lehmer against a plain Euclid twin on 200 fixed-seed cases.

**Deviations to review.** `Num` has no `Ord` (BUILDER §2 names one): comparing two big values multiplies and can refuse at the cap's edge, so `compare` returns a `Result`. `Big` has no derived `Ord` either; `Big::order` is a named call (R-118).

**For the lead.** `tools/trace.py`'s `P8_ID` is `P8-\d+[a-z]?`, so a fence's `PERF:` line can't name P8-M1..P8-M4; F4 (`num/**`) would name a micro row. No fence is open at G1.

**Next:** G2, foundations and the CLI's way in. **STOP-REPORTs:** none. **Exceptions:** +1, kernel `num/big.rs` `struct Big`, `clippy::disallowed_types` (R-29); count 5, report-only 0. **Check:** `check full: green, 30 steps (320s)`.

## STOP-REPORT 2026-11-01: rules-selftest's R-111/cli-over-budget assumes the CLI holds 2 exceptions; its sanctioned third makes it count 5

- **Kind:** a rule blocks correct code (the rule is right; its selftest case is stale)
- **Where:** `tools/rules-selftest/cases/R-111.case`, case `cli-over-budget` (last changed in 045355d); the code that trips it, `crates/tally-cli/src/main.rs` `fn read_reality`
- **Rule or gate:** R-111 (area budgets), R-39 and R-108 (the CLI's sanctioned I/O file); `tools/cli-io.toml` names the three CLI exceptions: "the arguments, the reality's files …, and the output"
- **What happens:** `engine/tools/check full` → `check full: red, 1 of 30 steps failed (317s)`, the one failure `FAIL R-111/cli-over-budget: the output lacks 'area cli has 4 exceptions, over its budget of 3 (R-111)'`. The case plants two exceptions in `tally-cli` ("Planted too: the CLI holds two since its stdin read went (audit C1)") and expects the count 4. G2 adds the CLI's third, the reading of the reality's files that INTERFACE §4 gives the binary (`#[expect(clippy::disallowed_methods, reason = "R-39: the reality's files are read here, once, before the pure run takes them")]`, lock written, `exceptions: … cli 3/3`), so the planted run counts 5 and prints `area cli has 5 exceptions, over its budget of 3 (R-111)`. The budget check itself works.
- **What I tried:** nothing that changes the count honestly. Folding the reading into `arguments` or `write_output` would hide an I/O call under another exception's reason; leaving the binary to pass an empty table would make it refuse every real path `LOAD-PATH`. Both are workarounds, so neither is done.
- **What I ask:** a rules-role fix to the case: plant one exception (the CLI now holds its three) and expect `area cli has 4 exceptions`, or keep two and expect 5. Then G2 commits as it stands.
- **State:** last green commit 5c29cf0. Uncommitted, all green but this step (`check fast` green, 12 steps; `check full` 29 of 30): G2, foundations and the way in: `crates/tally/src/base/code.rs` (the catalogue, 99 codes), `base/refusal.rs`, `api.rs` (the read step, `run`), `lib.rs`; `crates/tally/tests/catalogue.rs` (the builder's test against L §8 and E §11); `crates/tally-cli/src/lib.rs` (arguments, the file table, paths and directories, exits 0/1/2/74, usage), `main.rs` (reads the files); `EXCEPTIONS.lock` (+1, cli). The release binary prints M0's fixtures `LOAD-PATH/alone` and `LOAD-PATH/before-parse` byte for byte, exit 1.

**Resolved** 2026-11-01 by `eb91a56` (rules role, issue #28; the user: "Rules commit on build/s (Recommended)"): `R-111/cli-over-budget` now plants 4 and holds from any live CLI count. G2 commits as it stood.

## 2026-11-01 S, G3: the lexer and the parser (issue #26)

**Built.** `crates/tally/src/syntax/` (the lexer-parser area, budget 0), each file the twin of a part of refcore's `lexer.py` and `parser.py`, its error texts word for word: `lex.rs` (lines and their indentation, tokens: grouped numbers, dates, months, quarters, month-days, keys, currency codes, strings, operators); `tree.rs` (the tree: one enum per choice of the grammar, every node with its line); `parse.rs` (the cursor over one line's tokens, units, literals, spans, the depth count); `expr.rs` (expressions as flat chains, postfixes, atoms, binds, `bal`, `value`, `flows`, `covered`, aggregates, calendars, types); `item.rs` (the header and its checks, the sections, a version's lines, items and rows, the units prescan, `parse_reality`). `time/day.rs`: `Day`, a checked calendar day. `api::run` now splits every file, then parses every file (L §8.3), answers a parse refusal, and answers `NotImplemented` past the parse; `tally::parse`, the fuzz target's entry, runs the parser on one file.

**Green.** No T id of S's list. M0's fixture `LOAD-PATH/control` now prints byte for byte, exit 1 (with `alone` and `before-parse`, all three of M0's `LOAD-PATH` rows). X's 13 files (`b_r1`, and `b_r1s`'s asks) and all 312 realities of the kernel corpus parse, checked once with a temporary local test, not committed: the builder's committed tests read no T file (R-93). `crates/tally/tests/parse_cases.rs`: a reality in the corpus's forms parses to the expected sections, rows and asks; seven refusals print refcore's text (`LOAD-HEADER`, `LOAD-PARSE`, `LOAD-KNOWN`, `LOAD-RESERVED`, `LOAD-PLACEKIND`); a form built later is `NotImplemented`.

**Deviations to review.**
- Nodes carry their line number, not a byte span (BUILDER: a span on each node): refcore's messages name lines only. The interner and `Table<I, T>` come with the names they key, in G4.
- A construct the engine doesn't build yet stops the parse with `Stop::NotBuilt`, exit 69, never a guessed refusal: axes, fills, queries (`mean`, `share`, `quantile`), `low`, `crps`, `cdf`/`pdf`/`pmf`, distributions, `prorata`, `as if` (so `LOAD-ASIF` too), pins (`with` other than `with uncertain`), random asks and `random at`, the asks `explain`, `check`, `solve`, `diff`, `inputs-of`, `readers-of`, a version's `moves`, a kind's parts clauses, parts in a reading's row.
- `tally::parse` returns `Result<Vec<File>, Stop>` (it was `Result<(), NotImplemented>`); the fuzz target asks only `is_err()`, the stub bench only calls it.

**Spec gap, for the lead:** L §2.3's depth limit (`LOAD-DEPTH`, 8) counts brackets and `if`s; a read chained by `from` or `ties by` nests without either, so the grammar's recursion has no bound. The parser counts its own recursion there and stops at 64 with `NotBuilt` (R-16), not a refusal the spec doesn't name.

**Next:** G4, load and the read rule. **STOP-REPORTs:** none. **Exceptions:** +0; count 6; report-only 2, `lifetime-review` on the parser's cursor `P<'a>` (`syntax/parse.rs`, the struct and its `impl`): R-124's own case, a cursor over a text. **Check:** `check full: green, 30 steps (371s)`.

## 2026-11-01 S, G4c: the read rule (issue #26)

**Built.** `crates/tally/src/read/` (the kernel's, a plain scan; the row index is fence F3, opened when a budget row asks), refcore's `evaluate.py` its twin, messages word for word: `rows.rs` (the time point, a read with no law date, candidates: keys stated, `*` and ranges in index order, keys unstated that were there when the row was known, known by the cut, the span or instant; the choice: `replace` at the same keys, the narrower row, a span over `always`, ties `LOAD-OVERLAP`; `held`), `gap.rs` (no candidate: `none` outside a lifespan by the thing's membership hull, else `HOLE-UNGOVERNED`'s questions: the input's `question`, `no value for <period>`, `at <d>`, with no law date `no value`, a calendar's; a reading's plain read and `HOLE-NOREADING`; `read_input`), `rules.rs` (the version of a rule in force: `LOAD-OVERLAP`, `LOAD-LABEL`), `bands.rs` (the table a band read takes, refcore's recursion through earlier tables as a loop, `replace` rows patched in; the band's `held` mark and questions). A read returns the row, `none` or the question; the hole itself is built in the hole module (G5, R-116). `Lit::text` moved into the syntax tree (load's `lit_text` calls it). `crates/tally/tests/read_cases.rs`: 29 reads of a small reality and 5 tie refusals, each refcore's line from the same text (the refusals from the same change to refcore's loaded model, since its load refuses them first); a local probe, `tmp/s/refread.py`, prints refcore's lines.

**Green.** No T id.

**Deviations to review.**
- `last` and `since` are left for the evaluator: neither X nor the corpus reads them.
- A band `replace` table that stands as a whole table (no table under it, or rows that don't match the earlier intervals) is `NotBuilt`: telling it needs load's band checks (`whole_ok`), which are M0's.
- A dated read with no law date is `NotBuilt` (`LOAD-LAWDATE`, M0's), where refcore's interpreter guard raises.
- Cut classes come with the memo (G5), where they trim cells: an item's class is per its cone, which the item graph (G4d) gives. The calendar built-ins are `time/`'s (G4a); the evaluator binds them (G5).

## STOP-REPORT 2026-11-01: a leg whose place is a hole refuses ENGINE-INVARIANT in refcore, where L §5.14 says it books its known side

- **Kind:** other: the oracle and the spec disagree, on an input no T check reaches.
- **Where:** refcore `tools/refcore/walk.py:1234` (`whole = all(on for p, s, on in sides)`: an end whose place is a `Hole` counts as on, so the leg is taken as booked whole and only its known end joins `cons`); the engine's twin, `crates/tally/src/walk/day.rs` (`book_leg`, then `close_day`'s conservation check); the spec, L §5.14 Legs (l.1353: "the leg books its known side, and every read of the unknown side is a hole naming it") and E §9 invariant 1 (l.425: "the two ends of each leg booked whole sum to zero …; a leg booked by its ends … is left out").
- **Rule or gate:** E §9 invariant 1; DONE gate 10 (refcore's bytes); the skill's "never a crash: a broken invariant is a bug".
- **What happens:** the smallest input (`tmp/s/hp/reality.tally` in the worktree): places `acc_0`, `acc_1` (`accounts DUK`), `input dest: key accounts` with no row, `move mv_0: transfers` on 2025-01-10, `leg cash from acc_0 to dest amount amt` (`amt = 100.00`), openings 500.00 and 0.00, asks `bal(acc_0, 2025-01-31)` and `bal(acc_1, 2025-01-31)`. `python3 -I -B engine/tools/refcore run reality.tally` prints, exit 0, for both asks: `refused: ENGINE-INVARIANT conservation: the legs booked on 2025-01-10 in DUK sum to -100.00 DUK, not 0 (E §9 invariant 1)`; the engine (`target/release/tally run reality.tally`) prints the same bytes. By L §5.14 `a0` reads 400.00 and the unknown side's reads are holes naming `dest`; nothing in the spec refuses.
- **What I tried:** nothing in code: the engine keeps refcore's bytes (gate 10), and no corpus record or T check reaches a hole place (no `ENGINE-INVARIANT` in `tests/properties/kernel.refcore`).
- **What I ask:** the lead's ruling: either refcore leaves a leg with a hole place out of conservation, as invariant 1 leaves out a leg booked by its ends (then T adds a check, and the engine follows: one line in `book_leg`), or the spec says such a leg refuses, and with which code. What `bal` of a place that may be the unknown side reads (here `acc_1`) is the same question's second half.
- **State:** the last green commit is this G6 commit; nothing uncommitted under `engine/`.

a later t is `RUN-FUTURE`, d before t `RUN-DOMAIN`, t before S `HOLE-PRERUN`; its cut min(t, K); one walk per (walk continued, point, cut, moves left out), walked only as far as the read's day, as refcore's `run_to`; `left out:` for each move it leaves out with an instance dated in its window; `with uncertain`, the read's or the ask's. `walk/state.rs`: a projection's walk starts each place as the walk it continues stood at its point (`Projected`, the chain's states nearest first, the recursion counted by the chain's level). `walk/agenda.rs`: its plan pass reads no reading and makes no adjustment, counts the instances of the moves that depend on the holder instead of booking them, skips plans dated before t, keeps decision ranks global so points compare across walks, and on a decision's day keeps only the decisions after its point. `walk/day.rs`: `advance_to` (a walk paused after a day), the plan pass in its own collector, no `WARN-NEGATIVE` in a projection's walk. `walk/reads.rs`: a read of a projection's walk gathers the register and `left out:` of the walks it continues, while no reset is seen (refcore's `merge_cum`). `eval`: the holder of a projection (the ask, def, band or move whose expression is evaluated; the item graph gives what it leaves out), the ask's `with uncertain`. `crates/tally/tests/projection_cases.rs`: the builder's test, 16 asks of a small reality as refcore prints them, under both day sources (a def's projection read by a decision and by an ask, a decision's own projection, one from an earlier day, nested projections, a row known between t and K, `RUN-FUTURE`, `RUN-DOMAIN`, `HOLE-PRERUN`, a hole start, `with uncertain`, a projection below zero that warns nothing, a refusal after the read's day that refuses nothing). `crates/tally-cli/tests/walk_oracle.rs` now requires every corpus record answered.

- Fence F5 (the common case: start balance plus a prefix sum F) is not opened: R-130 and BUILDER §13.1 open a fence when its row asks, and P8-M4's bench is the lead's (R-91). Every projection is the plain walk of its window, which F5 will keep as its twin.

**Gap for the lead** (no check reaches it). When refcore cuts day t of a projection's walk at a decision's point, it compares the run's key `'<move>|%06d'` with each decision act's `<move>` as text, so of two decision moves in one stratum whose ids differ only by a suffix starting with a character before `|` (`sw`, `sw_b`), it drops the later one's act; the engine compares walk points (moves ranked by id) and keeps it, as L §5.14.1 says ("it counts only the movements not yet booked"). Two moves in one stratum read nothing the other changes, so only a hole or a refusal of the later one that holds for every place could show the difference.

## 2026-11-02 S, G10: the residual ledger (issue #26)

**Built.** Plan §3 G10, E §6.3 and L §5.18 `residuals`, refcore's `resid.py` (`m_residuals`) and `ask.py` (`answer_residuals`) the twins. `book/ledger.rs`: the ledger of a place over a span. The model at the ask's cut K: each instance of the plan at K (left-out ones and those with no status skipped) on a day a version covers the place, or fulfilled by an observation in the span; each decision on a covered day as the walk at K ran it; observations as known on the model's `on` date. Its lines: matched, expected but missing, unexplained, partly fulfilled; an adjustment's change, settled by an observation, against its version; zero lines; signs as flows into the place; a `balance` line for each reading in the span (booked = the walk at K just before the reading's point, gap = stated − booked, anchored or padded). Groups by month and `move <id>`, plus `?` (a day that is a hole), `unexplained` and `balance`, with their totals; holes first, then the biggest difference, the day, the group's name and the movement order, as refcore sorts them (a stable merge sort over fallible keys). `ask/residuals.rs`: the ask's head, group and line text, the register under it. `eval/stack.rs`: `residuals`, replayed on a deep stop like the other outermost reads. `walk/state.rs`: a place's state just before a point. `walk/agenda.rs`, `book/reading.rs`: a reading's point and its rank among the cut's readings. `crates/tally/tests/ledger_cases.rs`: the builder's test, four `residuals` asks of a small reality as refcore prints them, under both day sources (tenants' rent matched and missing, an uncertain grant paid in part, a fee whose day is a hole, a tip whose day an observation answers, an amount of 0, a January payment observed in February, a tax a later version raises with its change settled by an observation, a payment observed on an uncovered day, a sweep decision that moves 0, two unexplained observations, an anchored and a padded reading, `as known`).

**Green.** H2 `s_hh4_replay_september_at_b_r1`, H3 `s_hh4_replay_september_at_b_r1s` (R-0 onward). C2 `I:X:replay_september@b_r1`, C3 `I:X:replay_september@b_r1s`: the release binary's lines equal `tests/hh4/refcore/I_X_replay_september@b_r1.out` and `@b_r1s.out` byte for byte; C1 (konto_balance at b_r1) unchanged; checked by hand, not committed (R-93); run.py's count is the gate session's. ORA: all 312 records of `tests/properties/kernel.corpus` still equal `kernel.refcore` after the change below.

**Deviations to review.**
- A change outside the ledger: a movement's binding members are now read in a collector of each tuple's own, merged into its instance's register (refcore's `move_tuples`, `insts_of_tuples`; `eval/aggregate.rs` `binding_members`, `walk/plan.rs`). Before, a member's register reached whatever asked for the plan (in replay_september an `unfulfilled:` entry refcore doesn't print). The corpus was checked again after it.
- `NotBuilt` kept, M2's by BUILD-ORDER M2 ("the residual ledger in full"): a reading after a movement on its day; `bal(x, d) modelled` read by the ledger; `residuals … by f`; the ledger of a place of unit `any`. None of S's checks reaches them.
- Read sets per cell stay deferred (R-130): no G10 row needs them; P8-14, on replay_september, is measured at G11.
- T gap for the gate: partly fulfilled lines, the `?` group, adjustment lines, an observation fulfilling an instance outside the span and zero lines are reached only by the builder's test, not by T's S checks, so G11's mutants run may find survivors there that only the builder's test kills.

**Next:** G11, checkpoints, counters, fences and measurements (ORA on X's three checks, mutation on the kernel, P8-14 and PERFORMANCE §10's S unknowns). **STOP-REPORTs:** none new; issues #31 and #32 as decided (refcore's bytes at #31, the spec at #32). **Exceptions:** +0; count 6; report-only 5. **Check:** `check full: green, 31 steps (1536s)`.

## 2026-11-02 S, G11a: the counters behind `--stats`, invariants 2 and 5, P8-14's first readings (issue #26)

G11 is split in three: G11a (this entry), G11b the fences P8-14 asks for, G11c checkpoints, ORA on C1–C3, the remaining fences' headers, the `ARCHITECTURE.md` draft and the advisory kernel mutants.

**Built.**
- `counters.rs` (non-kernel; it exists only in the `counters` build, which needs debug assertions, R-51): P §1.1's units per ask's engine, gathered into the run's (`ask/answer.rs` `Answered`, `Seen`), a distinct read counted once across the asks, a count past `u64` printed `overflow`, never clamped. `--stats` prints `stats: ev= def_hits= def_misses= def_unwound= reads= distinct_reads= days_stepped= nonempty_days= walks= legs_booked= parts_groups=0 walks_shared=0 recomputed_locally=0 num_results=`, then `stats-defs:`, the twelve defs with the most misses. Counted where the work is done: `Engine::ev` (a node; a number or money made), `def_cell` (a hit, a miss, a cell unwound past the counted depth), `read_input_at` (a read, keyed by input, keys and context), `step_day` (a day opened, and whether it holds an act), `main_walk` and `projection` (a walk made), `book_leg_ends` (a leg booked). Parts groups, walks shared and cells recomputed locally stay 0 at S. `Answer::counters`, `Engine::counters`; the CLI's `--stats` prints the run's counts (`tally-cli` `counted`, `engine_run`). Every counting line is a `#[cfg(feature = "counters")]` statement, so the release build holds none of it.
- E §9 invariant 2 (refcore's `inv2_add`, `inv2_check` the twin): `walk/day.rs` `check_worth` at the day's close, after conservation: per unit, the day's change in the balances of the places that aren't outside equals the legs across the boundary and those booked by their ends, the pads from outside, the openings and the anchors' gaps; a hole amount, a lifespan hole or a balance that isn't known leaves the unit uncompared that day; a projection's walk isn't checked. `walk/state.rs` `Walk::inside`, `worth_add`; `unbook` returns the flow it took back, and `take_back` takes it out of the day's worth; `apply_opening`; `book/reading.rs` the anchor's and the pad's gap.
- E §9 invariant 5 (refcore's `inv5`): `walk/plan.rs` `Booked`, `check_booked`: a plan instance's existence and legs are the same under every cut that books it at the same booking cut, kept across a run's asks (`Engine::with_booked`, `take_booked`, carried by `ask/answer.rs`).
- A plan is shared, not copied: `move_instances` returns an `Rc<Vec<Inst>>`, and the readers that look for one instance clone only that one (`book/members.rs`, `observe.rs`, `ledger.rs`, `walk/reads.rs`). Release time barely moves (C2 52.5 s → 52.2 s); it gives back part of what the counters cost in the dev profile, where T's runner builds them (all features).

**Green.** No new T id; S stays 10/10. Checked once with the release binary, not committed (R-93): all 312 records of the kernel corpus equal `kernel.refcore` (`tmp/s/runcorpus.py`, `compare.py`), and C1–C3 equal `tests/hh4/refcore/I_X_*.out` byte for byte (`tmp/s/xcheck.py`), with both invariants on.

**Measured: first readings,** design runs by the builder, niced, one run each; the gate's timing run is the figure (PERFORMANCE §8).
- **P8-14** (`perf row P8-14 --runs 1`): wall 53.3 s, peak footprint 1 660 MB, 857 G instructions, against the proposed ≤ 1 s and ≤ 512 MB (refcore: 52.6 s, 4.95 GB).
- **P8-14's counts** (the counted debug binary on the row's recipe, run directly: `perf counts` stops at its 90 s timeout, and the debug run takes 263 s): ev 91 121 586; def hits 10 224 138; **def misses 2 511 856, over the proposed 2 270 000**; def cells unwound 2 339 584; reads 1 767 888, distinct 852 708; days stepped 926, of them non-empty 906; walks 21; legs booked 1 135; num results 53 268 537.
- **U-1:** about 585 ns a node all in (53.3 s over 91.1 M), where `shape.md` assumed 10–50 ns.
- **U-4:** at most about 660 B a cell (1 660 MB over 2.51 M cells, walks and plans included).
- **U-5:** 2.51 M misses, against refcore's ≥ 2.27 M (partial); the event walk steps 926 days in 21 walks.
- **U-12, U-16:** no `num::` function in the profile's top 120: the number tiers are no hot spot on P8-14 (U-12 itself needs a counter inside the kernel's pure functions, which hold no engine; its proxy is the profile).
- **samply 0.13.1 on macOS 26.6.2** records: `perf profile P8-14`, 53 242 samples.
- **Where the time goes** (inclusive): the keys of reads, `make_keys` 15.6 % and `to_key` 6.2 % (an index's position found by a linear scan of its keys, `IndexInfo::pos`, `added_by`); the memo's text keys, `cell_key` 10.7 %, `origin` 9.4 %, `keys_text` 3.4 %; whole instance lists cloned on every read of a plan, `Inst::clone` 9.8 %; registers cloned and dropped, `Collected` 5 % and set clones 3.3 %; scopes cloned, 3.2 %. Self time is in `memcmp`, `memmove` and `malloc`.

**What P8-14 asks (G11b).** Its wall time is 53× the proposed budget and its misses are over theirs, so it asks for fences (R-130, BUILDER §13.1), and first for structure that isn't clever code: plans shared instead of cloned; a typed memo key instead of a formatted text; an index's positions by bisection or computed from a range; registers shared. Then F2, the dense columns filled forward in key order (P §3.4, E §3.2), which takes the recurrences off the work stack: 2.34 M of the 2.51 M misses now unwind past the counted depth and replay. The order and the measurements to take after each step are in `tmp/s/g11-progress.md`.

**A margin to watch.** T's runner builds the engine with every feature, the counters included, and runs S's household tests in the dev profile under nextest's 300 s limit (`slow-timeout`, 60 s × 5). Without the counters, C2 takes 242 s in a dev build (niced); with them 263 s. The milestone report alone passes H2 and H3 at 252 s (konto_balance 223 s), but one `check full` this session timed both out at 300 s while the machine was busier (`S: 8/10`), and the run before and after it printed 10/10. Once S gates (after its tag), that is a flaky gate: G11b's speed-ups have to land before the gate, as P8-14 asks anyway.

**Not done in G11a:** the walk oracle on C1–C3 (one check in the dev profile takes about 4 minutes per walk source; it waits for G11b); checkpoints; the fences; `ARCHITECTURE.md`; the mutants.

**Next:** G11b, the fences P8-14 asks for. **STOP-REPORTs:** none new. **Exceptions:** +0; count 6; report-only 5. **Check:** `check full: green, 31 steps (1560s)`, `milestone-report: S: 10/10 green`.

## 2026-11-02 S, G11b: the memo's law-date classes and its typed key; P8-14 within its proposed budgets (issue #26)

**Found first.** A counted probe of the misses' contexts (not committed): `debt_before` had 480 cells, each computed at 480 law dates and 15 cuts, 808 066 misses. The move `loan_instalment` sets each instance's law date by `at inst_day[l, k]`, and the schedule's defs are `at reader`, so every instance recomputed the chain below it at its own law date. No walk point was ever in these cells' keys. The law date was the lever, ×112.

**Built.** E §3.2's law-date class, as a fence (R-130) in the memo's file, `crates/tally/src/eval/memo.rs` (non-kernel; `PERF:` P8-14; plain twin refcore, whose memo keys a cell by its exact law date, as `Memo::Exact` does here).
- **A typed key.** `CellKey` is (def, keys, cut, walk point), with its own explicit order (by key kind, then value, a movement by its stable id as the old text key had it); `CellId` adds the exact law date, for the cells in progress, the guarded ones and the work stack's unwound ones. The formatted text keys are gone from the def memo; `Ctx::key` is left only for the counted build's distinct reads.
- **A cell is kept for its class.** Each key keeps one cell per class reached, and a read finds the one whose class holds its law date. The class is narrowed while the cell computes: each open def cell has a frame (`open_class`, `close_class`), and every read that tests the law date narrows the innermost frame, when the read's law date is the cell's own. A read at another law date tells nothing about the cell's own date, since no law date is a value.
  - **Rows, rules and tables:** an input's rows and a def's givens by every row's span under the cut (`rows_class`), the rules in force by each version's, `v`'s and own span (`rules_class`), a band's tables by their versions' spans (`tables_class`). The class keeps to the side of every span boundary the day is on.
  - **The day alone:** a read with no candidate (lifespans; the question names the day), a reading, a calendar's days, a priced figure, a `held` row or table (the entry names the day read for), `RUN-CONFLICT` and `RUN-NO-RULE` (they name the law date).
  - **The ask's cut:** a row or table that may be held (a start and no end) is narrowed to the day's side of the ask's cut.
  - **Cells read inside:** a cell read at the same law date narrows the reader to the read cell's class. A walk-bound cell is kept at its own day.
  - **Cycles:** every `RUN-CYCLE` that turns on what is in progress (cells, plans, settlements, adjustments, links: one `narrow_open_to_days()` line at each of the five kernel sites in `walk/plan.rs`, `book/settle.rs`, `book/adjust.rs`, `book/observe.rs`) keeps every open cell at its own day.
  - **A check:** `Cells::keep` refuses `ENGINE-INVARIANT` for a class that doesn't hold its own day.
- **The twin's way in.** `tally::run_with_memo(given, request, memo)`; `run` is `Memo::Classes`. `Request` is unchanged, since the lead's bench `benches/p8_m4.rs` builds one. `ask::answer` takes the memo, and each ask's engine keeps its cells as it says (`Engine::with_memo`).
- **Tests.** Inline in `memo.rs`: spans split the law dates, the ask's cut, a kept cell's lookup, the key order, the invariant. `crates/tally/tests/memo_cases.rs`: one reality read from six law dates, where holes name their day, `held` entries name the day read for, a band's table, a rule version of 2027 and `RUN-NO-RULE` turn on it; refcore's 44 lines under both memos, and in the counted build fewer misses shared than exact. `crates/tally-cli/tests/walk_oracle.rs`'s second test runs the 312 corpus records under both memos: the same bytes (P §3.3's on/off check).

**Green.** No new T id; S stays 10/10. Checked with the release binary, not committed (R-93): C1–C3 equal `tests/hh4/refcore/I_X_*.out` byte for byte (`tmp/s/xcheck.py`, 0.7 s each); all 312 corpus records equal `kernel.refcore` (`runcorpus.py`, `compare.py`); `perf row P8-14` reports no wrong digest.

**Measured,** design runs by the builder, niced; the gate's timing run is the figure (PERFORMANCE §8).
- **P8-14** (`perf row P8-14 --runs 5 --warmups 1`): median 741 ms in one series and 834 ms in another, the same 11.55 G instructions (the machine's load); peak footprint 126 MB. Before: 53.3 s, 1 660 MB, 857 G. Proposed budgets: ≤ 1 s, ≤ 512 MB.
- **P8-14's counts** (the counted debug binary, 3.6 s; it took 263 s): ev 883 039 (91.1 M); def hits 63 142; **def misses 25 312** (2 511 856; proposed ≤ 2 270 000); unwound 0 (2.34 M); reads 64 969, distinct 33 323; days 926, non-empty 906; walks 21; legs 1 135; num results 277 113 (53.3 M). `stats-defs:` spend_day 3 392, debt_before 3 380 (480 keys × 7 cuts), overpay_paid 3 373, principal_part 3 373, inst_day 3 360, spend_month 1 168.
- **U-5:** misses 25 312 with law-date classes, about 2.51 M at each exact law date; the cut stays exact in the key.
- **The dev profile** (T's runner, all features): H1–H3 take 3.7 s each (223–252 s before), so G11a's margin to nextest's 300 s is gone as a risk. `check fast` takes 14–24 s (263 s) and `check full` 878 s (1 560 s).
- **Where the time goes now** (`perf profile P8-14`, `tmp/s/g11b-profile1.txt`): the ledger's walks and projections, `post` 90 % inclusive, `move_instances` 75 %, `main_walk` 61 %, `projection` 55 %. No memo function in the top 30 by self time but `CellKey::cmp` (1.9 %).

**Deviations to review.**
- E §3.2 defines the class statically, from the rows and rules the item's cone reaches. The engine's class is the intersection of the classes of the reads the cell actually made, at least as wide. P §3.3's test (two law dates share a cell only where its result, register included, is the same) holds by construction, and the twin checks it.
- A text that names the law date keeps its cell at that day. This covers a gap's question, `held`, `RUN-CONFLICT` and `RUN-NO-RULE`; refcore keys its memo by the exact law date, as G5b noted. E §3.2 l.153's "one hole, which names that day" is for a window `at`, which S doesn't build.
- **Not opened, because P8-14 now meets its three proposed budgets** (R-130: a fence only when a budget row asks): the index positions by bisection (`make_keys` is 1.1 % self), F2's dense columns (no unwinds left), registers shared by `Rc`, and E §2's cut classes. If the gate's run misses 1 s, the profile's next lever is the walk side (F5, checkpoints), not the memo.

**Next:** G11c, a fresh session (`tmp/s/g11b-progress.md`): ORA on C1–C3 as a committed test (a dev run of one check is now about 4 s), monthly checkpoints, each open fence's header, twin and test, the `ARCHITECTURE.md` draft, the advisory kernel mutants. **STOP-REPORTs:** none new. **Exceptions:** +0; count 6; report-only 5. **Check:** `check full: green, 31 steps (878s)`, `milestone-report: S: 10/10 green`.

## 2026-11-02 S, G11c: monthly checkpoints, the walk oracle on C1–C3, the open fences, the advisory kernel mutants; S built (issue #26)

**Built** (commit f7eba1b, then this entry's commit).
- **ORA on C1–C3** (BUILD-ORDER § S, Green): `crates/tally-cli/tests/walk_oracle.rs` runs X's three S checks, the 13 files of `cut11/b_r1` and `cut11/b_r1s` named as `checks.tsv` names them, under the event walk and the every-day walk: the same bytes (22.6 s in the dev profile). It reads X's files only, through `include_str!`, never a baseline (R-93, SQ-3). With the corpus test, the oracle now covers every S check.
- **Monthly checkpoints** (D2-6, P §2.4; kernel `walk/state.rs`): `Checkpoint` holds each (place, unit)'s state at the start of a month, an `Rc` shared with the checkpoint before where the state is the same, and the decision legs on the agenda from that day on (N-6). `Walk::keep_checkpoints` runs as `step_day` opens a day (before the day's acts leave the agenda) and as the walk ends: every first of a month after the last day walked, so a month the event walk skips is kept from the state the next day opens on. A projection's walk keeps none; parts and F5's structures don't exist at S. Nothing starts from a checkpoint at S: the forks (P §2.5) and the walk restart (P §4.2) come later (SQ-5). Inline tests: both day sources keep the same checkpoints; each equals the log's state just before its day (the plain twin, `state_before`); a place that doesn't move is one shared `Rc`; the legs booked ahead.
- **The open fences' `PERF:` lines, twins and tests** (R-130): F1 the event walk (`walk/agenda.rs`, P8-14; twin `oracle.rs`, test the walk oracle) and its checkpoints (`walk/state.rs`, P8-14's footprint; twin the log; test the inline ones); F2 the memo (`eval/memo.rs`, G11b's); F4 the number tiers (`num/small.rs`, P8-M1, twin the big tier; `num/gcd.rs`, P8-M2, twin refcore and the test's Euclid). F4's equivalence test is wider: `rational.rs` `the_tiers_agree_on_fixed_seed_values`, 600 fixed-seed pairs at 20, 40 and 63 bits, multiply, add, divide and compare directly and through the big tier. F3 (the read index) and F5 (the projection's prefix sums) stay closed: no row asks (P8-14 meets its proposed budgets; P8-M4 is measured on the plain walk).
- `lib.rs`'s header names every module S built. **`ARCHITECTURE.md` drafted** for the lead (R-119): the worktree's `tmp/s/ARCHITECTURE.md`, 203 lines: the bird's-eye view, the codemap by pipeline stage, the cross-cutting ideas, the invariants and what is absent on purpose, the boundaries, the tests, and "where the engine does X".

**Green.** No new T id; S stays 10/10. Checked with the release binary, not committed (R-93): C1–C3 equal `tests/hh4/refcore/I_X_*.out` byte for byte; all 312 corpus records equal `kernel.refcore`.

**Measured,** design runs by the builder, niced; the machine's load average was about 17 during the U-6 runs, so the wall figures are noise; the gate's run is the figure (PERFORMANCE §8).
- **U-6, the checkpoint interval, cost side** (P8-14, `perf row --runs 5 --warmups 1 --bin`, four release binaries): peak footprint 126.3 and 126.4 MB with no checkpoints, 127.2 MB weekly, 126.9 and 127.0 MB monthly, 126.8 MB quarterly; instructions 11.55–11.61 G for all four, a spread as large as the differences. X's run is 10.5 months, so 11 checkpoints a main walk; for 33 years that is 396 a walk, a state costing 168 B plus its `Rc` and key where it changed. The benefit side waits for forks and restarts (SQ-5).
- **U-15, the stack** (X's three checks on a thread of a given size; probe tests, not committed): the dev profile overflows at 448 KiB and passes at 512 KiB; `release-test` overflows at 128 KiB and passes at 256 KiB. No cell passes the counted depth (32) on P8-14 (0 unwound). T's runner gives a test 2 MiB, so S fits with 4× margin in its own profile; the thread module (M7) can size its stacks from this.
- **U-4, bytes per memo cell** (`size_of`, a probe test, not committed): a kept cell 160 B, the key 96 B, its `Vec` 24 B, so about 280 B inline, plus the heap of the def's name, the keys and the register (a `Collected` is 56 B inline, a `Value` 56 B, a place state 168 B).
- **U-3, read sets:** not measurable at S: read sets per cell aren't built (G8, R-130). Their nearest cost, the register every cell collects, is about 6 % of self time on P8-14 (`BTreeMap<Entry>` inserts 2.6 %, `Collected` drops 1.4 %, clones 1.0 %, merges 0.8 %; `tmp/s/g11b-profile1.txt`).
- **U-1:** 741 ms over 883 039 nodes, about 0.84 µs a node all in (walks, the ledger and allocation included; `shape.md` assumed 10–50 ns for the node alone).
- **U-11:** `Num` is 16 bytes (its test). Lehmer's share is E1's to measure (its constructs come later); P8-14's profile holds no `num::` function. **U-12, U-16:** as G11a (the profile's proxy; U-16 is the lead's two builds). **U-17, U-18:** the lead's and the gate's.

**The advisory kernel mutants** (`cargo mutants --in-diff` over G11's kernel diff, `daea4c8..f7eba1b`, with S's two test targets as if S gated, `--baseline skip` after the baseline passed, `-j 2`, niced; 7 minutes; `tmp/s/g11c-mutants/`): 82 mutants, **30 caught, 32 missed, 0 timed out, 20 unviable: 48.4 %.** The diff is mostly invariant checks and checkpoints, code S's T tests can't reach, so this figure isn't the kernel's; the gate's full run is (row 5). Triage:
- **Equivalent, 1,** listed in `MUTANTS-SURVIVORS.md`: `book_leg_ends`, `replace < with <=`: a booked end's sign is −1 or 1, never 0.
- **T gaps, 31,** none equivalent (a test can tell each from the code), for the lead:
  - *Checkpoints, 5* (`keep_checkpoints` ×4, `checkpoint` ×1): nothing reads one at S (SQ-5, as read).
  - *Invariant checks and the worth accounting only they read, 16:* `check_worth` ×2 (E §9 item 2), `check_booked` ×2 and `make_instances`'s `!mv.decision` (item 5), `Walk::inside` ×3, `book_leg_ends`'s `whole` ×2 (item 1) and its worth line ×2, `take_back`'s worth lines ×3, `apply_stated`'s pad worth ×1. On a correct engine they never fire, so only an injected fault tells them apart: gate 5's `ENGINE-INVARIANT` through the fault-injection hook, which the engine doesn't have yet (no site behind the `fault-injection` feature). The `whole` pair is also killed by issue #31's input (a leg whose place is a hole), which T doesn't have.
  - *The sign rule, 2:* `check_group → Ok(())`, `take_back → Ok(())`: no S check breaks the sign rule (L §5.10).
  - *Movements as values, 3:* `instance_by_id` and `fulfils_of` (`==` → `!=`): a read of one plan instance by id, or of an observation's `fulfils`, among two or more instances; `obs_links_for → Ok(vec![])`: an observation linked to the instance it fulfils, printed (L §5.19 Links).
  - *Readings, 1:* `apply_stated` (`==` → `!=`): one version stating readings of two places with different gap roles.
  - *Adjustments, 2:* `unsettled_adjustments → Ok(vec![])`, `settle_candidates`'s `!`: an unsettled adjustment a reader prints, a settlement move bound to adjustments.
  - *Decisions, 2:* `decision_runs` (`&&` → `||`, `!=` → `==`): flows of a decision move with an instance whose day is a hole.
- The builder's own tests (`ledger_cases`, `memo_cases`, the inline checkpoint tests) reach several of these, but gate 8 counts T's tests only (R-84).
- **For the lead:** `tools/mutants-survivors` keys a row by (file, function, mutation) without the position, so two mutants with one description in one function (`delete ! in Engine::book_leg_ends`, at two places) share a row: listing one as equivalent would also list the other.

**S is built:** every BUILD-ORDER § S item is in the code or deferred by G-S1 (`explain`, `low`, `next` to M1). MILESTONE-S rows 3 and 4 are green (`S: 10/10`); row 1's bytes equal refcore's with the release binary (run.py's own count is the gate's: the guard refuses it the build role); row 2 is refcore's, untouched; rows 5 (mutation) and 6 (timing) are the gate's. **Next:** the gate session (`engine-reviewer` on the diff, the oracle's run on every S check, row 5's gating question with the T gaps above, row 6's timing run; `ARCHITECTURE.md` from `tmp/s/`). **STOP-REPORTs:** none new; issues #31 and #32 as decided. **Exceptions:** +0; count 6; report-only 5. **Check:** `check full: green, 31 steps (838s)`, `milestone-report: S: 10/10 green`.

## 2026-11-02 S, G12: the gate's review fixes, read sets per cell (issue #26)

**The rulings** (the user, 2026-11-02, relayed by the coordinator). G-S2: "Read sets now, rest to M1 (Recommended)". B4: "Spec wins, fix after S": the ledger keeps its bytes (issue #33), no change here. O4 is issue #34, no change. T's gap for B1 is issue #35.

**Fixed** (the rows of `tmp/s/gate-review.md`).
- **B1, `with uncertain`** (L §5.14, §5.18; E §3.2). The clause is `Ctx::unc`, set only on the ask line's own context (`ask/answer.rs` `ask_body`; not on `as known` or `law`). Every timeline and decision context is made anew without it (`Ctx::new`), so no decision, plan, projection walk or binding filter inside a walk sees it. The engine-wide flag is gone; its readers take the context (`walk/projection.rs`, the read's own clause or the line's; `book/members.rs`, the binding filter). `CellKey` carries the clause, so a def read under it is a cell of its own. Builder test `tests/projection_cases.rs` `no_decision_sees_the_lines_with_uncertain`, under both day sources: a sweep decision reads a def's projection and an uncertain bonus follows; with refcore the sweep books 900.00 DUK under every ask, while the G11c binary booked 1 400.00 under the ask that states the clause.
- **B2, E §9 item 4** (asks move no money): `crates/tally/src/invariant.rs`, the file `tools/kernel.toml` names (protected, non-kernel). A main walk's signature, every entry of every place's log (point, change, movement), is kept when the walk is done (`walk/day.rs`); after each ask, every walk not failed is compared with it, the `as known` engine's and the line's, and a change refuses `ENGINE-INVARIANT asks move no money: the timeline at cut K changed while the ask was answered (E §9 invariant 4)`. A projection's walk keeps none. Inline tests. Items 1 and 2 stay in `walk/day.rs`, item 5 in `walk/plan.rs`; 3 and 6 aren't S's.
- **B3, read sets per def cell** (E §3.2: "the rows and cells it read, band rows included"; P §4: built now). `eval/memo.rs`: `Read` is an input read at its keys, a row chosen (version and stable id), a band read at its key, a band row chosen, or a def cell read; `Cell.reads` keeps them sorted, each once. Recorded in `read_input_at`, `given`, `band_call` and `def_cell` (a hit, a miss, a cycle). A cell's set holds what its own evaluation read, the cells it read but not what those read. A walk, a plan, adjustments and links are shared by every reader, so their reads are theirs: each opens a read set that keeps nothing (`walk/day.rs`, `walk/plan.rs`, `book/adjust.rs`, `book/settle.rs`, `book/observe.rs`). **A reading for the gate:** a cell's dependence on a walk or a plan is not in its read set; P §4.2's walk restart and E §5's plan pass own those. Inline test `a_cells_read_set_holds_what_it_read_and_not_what_its_cells_read`.
- **O1:** `base/table.rs`'s header says what it is, `Table<T>` keyed by the name's text.
- **O2:** cited rather than refused. A def's `at` and its key binds' `where` are held by the def (`eval/rules.rs` `read_def_at`), and a move's `at`, bindings and body by the move (`walk/plan.rs` `make_instances`): L §5.14.1 ("the item holding the read: in a def …"; "the def that holds it, never … who reads the def"), and refcore's holder is the item whose text holds the read. Before, both took the outer reader's holder.
- **O3:** `read/rules.rs` `overlap()` refuses a `replace rule` with no version `ENGINE-INVARIANT`, never the text `?`.

**Deferred, by G-S2:** to M1, the interner (D2-3), `Table<I, T>` (D2-4) and the context's layer slots (D1-3). F2's dense columns and F3's read index and read memo stay closed (R-130): P8-14 meets its proposed budgets without them.

**Measured, U-3** (read-set overhead on a cold run, P §10), P8-14, `perf row --runs 5 --warmups 1`, niced, the G11c binary (sha `1fd366ab`, the gate's) and this one alternately, two series each: wall 784.8 and 785.2 ms → 832.8 and 833.1 ms (**1.06×**); instructions 11.55 G → 11.88 G (**1.03×**); peak footprint 126.4 and 127.1 MB → 154.1 and 154.5 MB (**1.22×**). 1.0.1 cited 1.27×. B1 and B2 add next to nothing to these. **U-4 with read sets:** about 1.1 KB more per kept cell (27.7 MB over P8-14's 25 312 misses), since each `Read::Cell` holds the read cell's key, its def name and keys on the heap. A first form, one `BTreeSet` per cell, peaked at 179.5 MB (a tree node per cell, even for one read); the kept form is a sorted vector. All three readings are inside P8-14's proposed budgets (1 s, 512 MB), so nothing smaller is asked (R-130); an id per kept cell, with M1's interner, would cut the bytes.

**Green.** No new T id; S stays 10/10. Checked with the release binary, not committed (R-93): C1–C3 equal `tests/hh4/refcore/I_X_*.out` byte for byte; all 312 corpus records equal `kernel.refcore`.

**Next:** the gate's second review. **STOP-REPORTs:** none new. **Exceptions:** +0; count 6; report-only 5. **Check:** `check full: green, 31 steps (876s)`, `milestone-report: S: 10/10 green`.

## 2026-11-02 S, G13: the gate's second-pass fixes N1–N4 and B3(a), and N5 (issue #26)

**Fixed** (the rows of `tmp/s/gate-review.md`'s "Second pass"; T's gaps for N1–N3 are issue #37).
- **N1, a member whose day is a hole** (L §5.19 Bindings; refcore `mreads.py:513`). `book/members.rs` `sort_key` keys a hole day, or none, as 9999-12-31 (`Day::LAST`, refcore's `datetime.date.max`), so such a member comes after every dated one. Before, `None` sorted first.
- **N2, a refused leg read in a projection's walk** (E §4.2 step 5, §4.3; refcore `mreads.py:154-158`). `walk/reads.rs` `leg_refused` looks in the walk and then in each walk it continues, the nearest first (`chain_refusal`, its loop bounded by the walks' count, as `read_state`'s is). Before, a projection's walk saw only what it had refused itself.
- **N3, `x in K on d`** (L §5.7, §8.4; refcore `evaluate.py:2394-2397`). `eval/expr.rs`: a `d` that is a hole makes the test that hole; anything else goes through `ask::date_of`, so `none` and `never` refuse `RUN-NONE` with refcore's text and a day period stands for its day. Before, any value that wasn't a day became the test's result.
- **B3(a), a def's givens in its read set** (E §3.2; P §4.1 step 2). `eval/memo.rs` has a new read, `Read::Givens { def, keys }`. `eval/rules.rs` `given` records it whenever a def with given rows is read at its keys, whether or not a row is chosen, as `Read::Input` is recorded for an input. B3(b) is the user's ruling (issue #36, M1): walk and plan reads aren't recorded.
- **N4:** `invariant.rs`'s header now says item 6 holds by the walk's `RUN-LIFESPAN` refusals (`walk/day.rs`), and item 3 is scenarios', not built at S. **A correction to G12's entry:** its "3 and 6 aren't S's" is wrong for item 6, which the walk holds. Only item 3 isn't S's.
- **N5, found by the builder, not a gate row** (L §5.18, §8.2; refcore `ask.py:431-438`, `:464-465`, `evaluate.py` `members` with `regs`). In an ask's `for` over movements, each tuple's line now carries the registers of the movements it binds, and only theirs. Before, they went into the ask engine's outermost collector and were lost, so `members (m = tip): 40.00 DUK` printed without `unfulfilled: tip`. `eval/aggregate.rs` `members(…, Brings)` takes `ToReader` for an aggregate (as before) or `ToTuple` for an ask's tuples (`eval/stack.rs` `tuples`). The register is kept in `Member.brought` and merged into the line (`ask/answer.rs`). N1's test needs this, since each member's line prints its register.

**Tests** (the builder's; the expected lines are refcore's run of the same text, and the realities are in the worktree's `tmp/s/g13/`): `tests/ask_cases.rs` `movement_members_come_by_day_a_hole_day_last_each_line_with_its_own_register` (N1, N5; both day sources) and `a_membership_tested_on_a_value_that_is_no_day_is_refused` (N3); `tests/projection_cases.rs` `a_projection_keeps_the_legs_the_walk_it_continues_refused` (N2; both day sources); `eval/memo.rs` inline `a_defs_givens_are_in_its_read_set_whether_or_not_one_is_chosen` (B3(a)); `time/day.rs` inline, `Day::LAST`. Each was reproduced before its fix: G12's binary prints the hole-day member first and no member registers, `50.00 DUK` for the projection where refcore refuses `RUN-LIFESPAN`, and `never` and `none` as the membership tests' values.

**Found, not fixed: N6, for the gate and the lead.** `flows` of a movement (`walk/reads.rs`, the loop over instances) gathers its register and hands it to the reader only after the loop. So a refusal met inside the loop (the instance's error, a refused amount, a refused leg) drops what was met before it. refcore registers as it goes (`self.reg`, `self.cur.merge`), so its refused line keeps it. The smallest input is `tmp/s/g13/n6.tally`: N2's reality with the decision reading `bal(bank)` and the ask `flows(match, 2025-02-01 .. 2025-03-31)`. refcore prints `matched: refused: RUN-LIFESPAN pay.cash moves into or out of old outside its lifespan`, then `  unfulfilled: match` and `  cut 2025-03-10`; the engine omits the `unfulfilled:` line. G12's binary does the same, and no T check reaches it. Other reads that gather and then add may share it; I haven't surveyed them. It is kernel code beyond the gate's rows, so it waits for the coordinator.

**Green.** No new T id; S gates since 060451c. Checked with the release binary, not committed (R-93): C1–C3 equal `tests/hh4/refcore/I_X_*.out` byte for byte; all 312 corpus records equal `kernel.refcore`; the three realities above equal refcore's lines.

## 2026-11-02 S, G13b: N6, a refused read keeps the register it met before the refusal (issue #26)

**The coordinator's word** (2026-11-02): N6 is inside S's scope and differs from refcore, so it is fixed now. T's gap goes into issue #37.

**Fixed** (L §8.2, §8.4; E §4.2 step 5). refcore registers into the line's collector while it counts (`self.reg`, `self.cur.merge`), so a refusal met partway keeps what came before it. Three of the walk's reads gathered their register first and handed it to the reader only at the end, so a refusal dropped it:
- **N6, `flows` of a move** (L §5.14.3; refcore `mreads.py` `m_flows_move`). `walk/reads.rs` `flows_move` now counts in `count_move`, and the register is handed over before the count's refusal propagates. A refused run's `unfulfilled:` and every earlier run's now print under the refused line.
- **The same divergence in two other reads, found while fixing N6:** `flows` of a place (L §5.14.3; `m_flows_place`). `Sums::logs` reads the global log and the place's logs, and on a refusal what they met is kept; the adjustment entries still come only after the logs, as in refcore. And `bal(x, d) with uncertain` (L §5.14; `m_with_uncertain`): `Uncertain::movements`, so a refusal keeps the `uncertain:` entries before it. `walk/reads.rs`'s header says so.
- Looked at and left: `book/observe.rs` `obs_links_for`, whose refcore twin pushes its own collector and drops it on a refusal, as the engine does; a def cell keeps and passes on what it met with a refusal already (`eval/rules.rs` `def_cell`).

**Tests** (the builder's; refcore's lines; both day sources; the realities in the worktree's `tmp/s/g13/`): `tests/ask_cases.rs` `a_refused_flows_of_a_move_keeps_what_it_met_before` (n6.tally: a decision run refused alone, and a keyed decision whose second run is refused after the first moved 0) and `a_refused_flows_of_a_place_or_with_uncertain_keeps_what_it_met_before` (n7.tally). Each was reproduced before the fix: G13's binary prints the refused lines with only `cut`.

**Green.** No new T id. Checked with the release binary, not committed (R-93): C1–C3 equal `tests/hh4/refcore/I_X_*.out` byte for byte; all 312 corpus records equal `kernel.refcore`; n1, n2, n3, n6 and n7 equal refcore's lines.

## 2026-11-02 S, G13c: T1, a projection's walk counts the decision runs of the walks it continues (issue #26)

**Fixed:** T1, the blocking row of `tmp/s/gate-review.md`'s third pass (L §5.14.1, §5.14.3; E §4.3; refcore `mreads.py:1459-1462`). `walk/reads.rs` `decision_runs` finds each instance's run in the walk, then in each walk it continues, the nearest first (`chain_run`, bounded by the walks' count as N2's `chain_refusal` is). The `limit` applies to the run found. **What.** The 16 kernel mutants T's notes class as dead (`tmp/t-close/survivors-by-group.tsv`, class `dead` and `dead (plan)`): 11 T found in chunks 2–7 and the plan's 5. - `book/reading.rs` `Gap::anchor`, the field, and the line computing it in `apply_stated` (1, `role == Anchor` → `!=`): nothing read the field. The worth check matches on the role, and the ledger's balance line asks the version (`ledger.rs` `roles`).

**Kept (2 sites, 7 mutants), equivalents for the listing step:**
- `walk/state.rs` `Walk::keep_checkpoints` (4: → `Ok(())`; the guard `self.start.day() == 1` → true, → false; `==` → `!=`) and `Walk::checkpoint` (1: `==` → `!=` in the sharing filter). BUILD-ORDER § S builds them: S's row lists "checkpoints", and § S's walk item says "Monthly checkpoints are copied-on-write place states (D2-6); their interval is an unknown measured here" (U-6, G11c). Reason: "R-87: equivalent: S builds the monthly checkpoints (D2-6, P §2.4), but nothing at S starts from one: forks (M2) and the walk restart (P §4.2) come later (SQ-5). No ask reads a checkpoint (only the inline test calls `checkpoint_state`), so no output can tell a changed, missing or extra checkpoint apart." For `checkpoint`'s `==` → `!=`, also: sharing the previous checkpoint's `Rc` or making a fresh copy holds an equal state.
- `walk/point.rs` `At::text` (2: → `String::new()`, → `"xyzzy"`). T found no caller, but the `counters` build calls it: `eval/state.rs` `Ctx::key`, itself `#[cfg(feature = "counters")]`, names a running read's point in `--stats`' distinct reads (P §1.1). BUILD-ORDER § S asks for the counters. The method is now `#[cfg(feature = "counters")]` like its only caller, so the default build no longer carries it. Reason: "R-87: equivalent: `At::text` is compiled only in the `counters` build (`--stats`, R-51), where it names a running read's point in the distinct-read count; the mutation run builds without that feature, so the mutated function is not in the code the tests run."

**Made test-only:** none. Each test-only helper was deleted and its unit test adjusted instead.

**Score.** The 9 deleted mutants leave the denominator. T's counts assumed the plan's 5 deleted as well, and those 5 stay. The 7 kept become rows of `MUTANTS-SURVIVORS.md` in the listing step, from the final CI run's list. `MUTANTS-SURVIVORS.md` is not edited here.

## 2026-11-03 S, row 5: every survivor listed in `MUTANTS-SURVIVORS.md` (issue #41)

**What.** `engine/MUTANTS-SURVIVORS.md` lists every kernel survivor under R-87 as amended in f64b940. The source is T's `tmp/t-close/final-survivors.tsv`: 215 mutants, 132 equivalent and 83 missed. Each row's function is the one in CI run 41584287870 (at 6e6adae). One row per key (file, function, mutation): **196 rows, 102 `R-87: equivalent:` (109 mutants) and 94 `R-87: missed:` (106 mutants)**. Same-key lines were merged: the same mutation at two columns, or in one function on two lines. The 7 I kept in c3b4769 (`keep_checkpoints` ×4, `checkpoint`, `At::text` ×2) are T's rows already, not doubled. The file's one earlier row (`book_leg_ends`, `<` → `<=`) is T's `day.rs` 522 key and is listed once. Missed rows read `not built at S: …`, `blocked: refcore disagrees with the spec, #43: …` or `open: <the recipe>`.

**Every equivalent read against 6e6adae's code.** I accept 109 of T's 132. Claims checked at the code, with the code behind each:
- Lehmer's gcd (8): every mutant gives u and v the same leading bits or none, so the quotient bounds disagree and the step falls back to one Euclidean division. The mask on line 151 keeps `limb` → 1 and `&` → `|` in a word.
- `Big::pow`'s extra squaring: uncapped, same value.
- `check_cap` `>` → `<`: the exact comparison with 10^4096 refuses what the screen would.
- The tiers in `money.rs` and `rational.rs`: the big forms compute the same exact values and demote on return.
- `parse_decimal` and `from_decimal_digits`: the lexer's `number` and `grouped` hand them only digits with an optional point and a digit after it.
- `Num::round`: eval refuses a step that isn't positive first (RUN-DOMAIN).
- `Num::ratio`: its one library caller passes 10^k.
- `covered_call`: each step is a holding span's end, so any choice advances and the answer is the same.
- `sort_adjustments`: register entries are a `BTreeSet`; hole questions merge as a set; `adjustment_line`'s last match is within one move's list, which comes in order.
- `self_tie` over one run: a day's runs are cleared per act, so there is no pair.
- `schedule_decision` 532 (8): the per-move left-out count is read only through a place's legs.
- The invariant checks (`check_worth`, `check_booked`): the user's decision (b).

**Moved from equivalent to missed (23 mutants), for T:**
- **`run_decisions` 85, `>` → `>=` and `&&` → `||`.** With one due instance of a move whose `ties by` is a hole, refcore runs it (it orders only more than one instance, `walk.py` `tie_order`'s caller), and the mutant refuses RUN-TIE. Line 99's `>` → `>=` shares the first key, so it is counted with it, though alone it would be equivalent.
- **`make_pad` 397 (2), not built at S.** A converted `flows` of a place over a pad (L §5.14.3) rounds each movement's amount, at least 0, before its direction. The mutant writes the pad the other way with a negative amount, which rounds to another minor unit under `floor` or `ceil`. S answers a converted `flows` "not implemented".
- **The rank family (17).** T's reason ("ranks only order acts inside one step … booked together") misses that a place keeps the **first** refusal booked at it (`walk/state.rs` `apply`, `Change::Bad`). So the order of two refused movements at one place in one step shows in `bal`. Two more facts make the order differ:
  - An order text is `move|keys`, and `ab|…` sorts before `a|…`, while move `a` comes before `ab`.
  - Observations come sorted by (day, id), but their legs carry their own days.

  The mutants: `schedule_observations`' rank, `leg_text` ×2, and `obs_rank` `==`, `>`, `Ok(0)`, `Ok(1)`; `schedule_plan`'s `==` → `!=` and its rank; `book_run`'s later-leg rank; `text_rank` ×7. Each row gives its recipe, e.g. plan moves `a` and `ab` with legs at P on one day whose amounts refuse with different codes, then `bal(P)` that day.

  Kept as equivalent in that family: `obs_rank`'s `<` → `<=`, which moves every rank by 1, since every text is in the list. `schedule_modelled`'s rank, which only orders marks into a register set. `reading_point`'s stratum. `batch_end`'s and `closing_end`'s fields.
- **`rank_le` `<=` → `>`.** Its key covers line 212 (equivalent, T's reason) and lines 213–214 (not built at S), so the row is `missed`.

## STOP-REPORT 2026-11-03: the survivors scorer can't read a row whose mutation holds a `|`

- **Kind:** other: a tool (rules-owned) can't parse a valid row.
- **Where:** `engine/tools/mutants_survivors.py` line 90, `cells = [c.strip() for c in line.strip().strip("|").split("|")]`. 30 of `engine/MUTANTS-SURVIVORS.md`'s 196 rows hold a `|` in cargo-mutants' description, such as `replace || with && in Num::parse_decimal` or `replace | with ^ in leading_bits`. They are written in Markdown's escaped form, `replace \|\| with && in Num::parse_decimal`.
- **Rule or gate:** R-87, DONE gate 8.
- **What happens:** `engine/tools/mutants-survivors tmp/mutants/41584287870/mutants-shard-*` exits 1. Each such row splits into more than 4 cells and is skipped, so its survivors are reported "not in MUTANTS-SURVIVORS.md" and its equivalents aren't subtracted. The line is `score 89.4% … - equivalent 90`, against 90.7 % and equivalent 110 when cells split only at unescaped pipes. The same holds for the CI job.
- **What I tried:** the key is cargo-mutants' description, so it can't be reworded. A raw `|` breaks the table too. No row form that both renders as Markdown and passes this parse holds a pipe.
- **What I ask:** the rules role amends the parse to split cells only at unescaped pipes, `re.split(r"(?<!\\)\|", …)`, and unescape `\|` to `|` in each cell. The docstring and the file's header should say so.
- **State:** the last green commit is this entry's, with the listing. Nothing uncommitted.

## 2026-11-03 S, row 5: the gate's review of 58270b7, items 4.1 and 4.2 (issue #41)

No change in behaviour.

1. **`book/reading.rs`'s comments:** the header's invariant and `apply_stated`'s doc no longer say the gap is kept for the ledger. A gap is used where it is met (worth, the register, a pad's amount), and the ledger's balance line computes its own from the reading.
2. **`booked_coll`:** now built only in the pad arm, the one place that reads it. It can't refuse there (`self.walk(id)?` already passed), and `Walk::cum` only extends a cached prefix. Between the old call and the pad arm, only worth and the `inside` cache change, so the pad's register is the same.
3. **The last entry's claim about later milestones:** corrected there. M2 builds "statements with their anchors, pads and gaps" and the residual ledger in full (BUILD-ORDER.md:162). At M2 it rebuilds what it needs: a reading's gap per statement, and the pads as movements of `pads` that the ledger's lines and bindings over pads can read.
4. **`walk/point.rs` `At::same_act`, "delete field `sub`"** (CI 192:39 and 192:14): moved from equivalent to `R-87: missed: open`.
   - A decision's own observation is booked at sub-step 1 (`book_obs_now`), in the decision's group. When the sign rule refuses that group at sub-step 2, the code takes back the observation's entry and its register (`unbook`). The mutant at column 39 matches only sub-step 0, so the entry stays.
   - Both ends are refused from sub-step 2 either way, as the gate saw. But a refused `bal` still takes in the register met before the refusal (`reads.rs`, `absorb` before `state_value`, the N6 rule). An observation's register holds what judging its link met (`observe.rs`, `pop_alone`, e.g. `assumed`). So with the mutant, `bal(Q)` can print a line the code doesn't.
   - The row gives the recipe. Column 14 alone would be equivalent (the take-back's point is at sub-step 0), but it shares the key.

## STOP-REPORT 2026-11-04: #55's four survivors are a test gap: a day's plan order shows in which refusal a place keeps

- **Kind:** a test seems wrong (a missing vector); the spec's claim in E §4.1 is too strong.
- **Where:** `walk/plan.rs` `Order::text`, `quoted`; read by `walk/agenda.rs:198`, `:428` (`schedule_plan`, the plan's `At.rank`); `walk/state.rs` `apply`, `Change::Bad` (a place keeps the first refusal booked at it). The same gap is already listed: `MUTANTS-SURVIVORS.md` rows 124 (`delete field rank … in schedule_plan`) and 126 (`== → !=` in `schedule_plan`), both `R-87: missed: open:` with the `a`/`ab` recipe; T never added the vector.
- **Rule or gate:** DONE gate 8 (R-87, issue #55); E §4.1, last paragraph ("the order changes no balance and no refusal").
- **What happens:** The text rank is live, not dead code and not equivalent. A day's plan legs are booked in order-text order (refcore's walk key `osort`, `<move>|<keys tuple>`). Typed order would differ in two cases: move ids where one is a prefix of the other (`ab|…` sorts before `a|…`, since `b` comes before `|`), and a key place of 10 against 2. When two plan legs on one day refuse at one place with different messages, that order decides which refusal `bal` prints. With every rank tied (`Order::text` mutated), the stable sort falls back to the typed movement order, so the other refusal prints. Probe 1, the smallest:

```
model order_probe
  tally 2
  on 2025-12-31
  start 2025-01-01
  end 2025-12-31

source fixture "probe (invented values)"

unit DUK: currency minor 0.01

input amt: money DUK

input d0: date

place src: accounts DUK lives 2025-01-01..

place gone: accounts DUK lives 2025-01-01..2025-01-05

move a: transfers
  on d0
  leg cash from src to gone amount amt

move ab: transfers
  on d0
  leg cash from src to gone amount amt

ask q: bal(src, 2025-01-31)

version facts
  cite fixture
  governs always
  known 2025-01-01
  amt = 1.00 DUK
  d0 = 2025-01-10
  opening[src] = 500.00 DUK at 2025-01-01 start
  opening[gone] = 0 DUK at 2025-01-01 start
```

  refcore and the engine both print `q: refused: RUN-LIFESPAN ab.cash moves into or out of gone outside its lifespan`. With `Order::text → String::new()` built (`check step release-binary`), the engine prints `a.cash …`. By the same tie, it also kills `Order::text → "xyzzy"` and rows 124 and 126 (`==` → `!=` ranks both plans by move id: `a` and `ab` both 0, so typed order).
  `quoted` needs a second probe: with single keys its mutants are equivalent, because ids are identifier characters, which all sort after the closing quote. Probe 2 is probe 1 with the moves replaced by one move of two keys, `index flats = [f1, …, f11]` and places `x`, `y`:

```
move m[p in accounts on d0, f in flats]: transfers
  on d0
  when (p == x and pos(f) == 3) or (p == y and pos(f) == 11)
  leg cash from src to gone amount amt
```

  with `opening[x]`, `opening[y]` = 0 DUK. refcore and the engine print `RUN-LIFESPAN m[x, f3].cash …`. With `quoted → "xyzzy"` built, the engine prints `m[y, f11].cash …`: the ids tie, so the key places' text decides (`(0, 10,` before `(0, 2,`). Both probes are at `tmp/cleanup/order_probe.tally` and `quoted_probe.tally` in this worktree.
- **What I tried:** Read E §4.1 and refcore's walk keys (`walk.py` `sched_leg`, `osort`). Built each mutant once (`Order::text → String::new()`, `quoted → "xyzzy"`), ran both probes, then restored `plan.rs` byte for byte and rebuilt. The tree is clean and the release binary is the real one again.
- **What I ask:** One of two, the master's call:
  (a) T adds both probes as kernel vectors with refcore's bytes. That kills the 4 mutants and rows 124 and 126; I resume and confirm with `mutants-local`.
  (b) The spec round rules which refusal a place keeps when two plan legs of one day refuse there. E §4.1 says the order "changes no refusal", which holds for the set of refused legs but not for the printed message. If the ruling is "the first in the typed movement order", refcore's `osort` changes, and the text rank (`Order::text`, `quoted`, `order_texts`, `text_rank`) is deleted.
  Not chosen: listing the 4 as `R-87: missed: open:` like rows 124 and 126. That would green gate 8 without a test, and the brief's case 3 says stop.
- **State:** Last green commit 5752953 (master); nothing uncommitted but this report. #53 items 1, 2, 4 and 5 are not started (item 3 waits on this ruling).
- **Resolved 2026-11-04.** The user ruled "Both: T now, spec later". T's 4c45536 adds g6/refusal_order and g6/refusal_keys, each with its twin, as s_kernel vectors with refcore's bytes; the spec question is #57. T's `mutants-local -F 'Order::text|quoted|schedule_plan'` on `plan.rs` and `agenda.rs` caught the 4 #55 survivors and rows 124 and 126, which leave `MUTANTS-SURVIVORS.md` in the next commit. Its 13 missed are all listed rows (112, 121, 122, 125, 127, 163, 164, 176–180). The text rank stays until #57 rules.

## 2026-11-04 M0, G2: the formatter, `reprint` and the hash (issue #56)

- **Built:** refcore's `canon.py` and `cli.reprint_paths`, as four new files in `syntax/`:
  - `canon.rs`: each file reprinted, and the hash. The header's order, the sections by kind then id, versions last by `known`, kinds in tree order (`kahn`), the body ranks, the short and long def forms (2.1-4), sorted rules and version items, a sorted `beats`.
  - `canon_order.rs`: the declarations table (`LOAD-KEY`, `LOAD-NAME`) and the sort components `Keys`, `Tie`, `Rev` and a member's key. A member's keys that differ stop "not built" until G9.
  - `spacing.rs`: `spaced`, and the numbers' canonical text (a reduced fraction included).
  - `parens.rs`: the paren pass.
- **Token roles and paren positions** (moved over from G1): each token carries a `Mark` in a `Cell`, set by the parse of its line: its role, whether it was read as a name, its canonical text, a plain or forced `(`, and the forced pairs to add. It also carries `seq`, its place as split, which names it after others are taken out.
- **The paren pass:** a pair goes when the section parsed without it is the same tree. A blind parse (no `Paren` node, no source text) stands in for refcore's `sig`. Because refcore nests `+ - * / and or` to the left, a blind parse joins a chain whose first term is a chain of the same operator, so `(x + y) + z` loses its pair as in refcore. `format/parens_redundant` was red until that.
- **Entry points:** `tally::reprint` (files read, printed and hashed in full-path order, each printed under its name); the CLI's `reprint` (a file named as given, a directory's files relative to it); `tally::format_file` for the fuzz target `format` (a rules step).
- **Own tests (not gating):** `tests/format_cases.rs`, with refcore's reprint of a small reality (its sha256 line too) and the canonical text as a fixed point; `reprint`'s names and order; numbers and gcd in `spacing.rs`.
- **Green ids (32):** every `m0_format` test (all 12 corpus groups, the joint households E1 and E2 included, and the 19 L §2.4 fixtures) and `m0_format_idempotent`. UL-09 and UL-14 print as refcore 2.1.3 does, so the plan's SR-2 hold isn't needed.

  - `m0_format_equal_models_print_identically` (and the other `law_holds` tests): of its 176 failures, 80 are the load probe (`REQ-ASK`, G3's SR-1) and 16 a base that must load clean (G3 on). The other 80 are the STOP-REPORT below. Every engine reprint equals refcore's: no failure is a reprint.
- **No mutants-local:** syntax/ isn't kernel.

- **State:** the code is unchanged by this report. G2 is committed with it; the four tests stay red, also for load (G3 on).

## 2026-11-04 M0, G2 fix: the `format` fuzz target's ENGINE-INVARIANT (issue #56)

- **The finding** (the rules step's 7f0d04f, `tmp/m0-rules/format-invariant.tally`): two rows of one cell, `x[c] = 0 governs 2026` and `x[c] = 1 governs ab`, made `format_file` refuse with `ENGINE-INVARIANT`. The sort compared a span start that is no date with a day as two components "of different forms".
- **refcore:** its reprint crashes on the same comparison (`REFCORE-NOT-YET … TypeError`). Its loader refuses such a span start as `LOAD-PARSE cannot parse: expected a date or a period, got ab`.
- **Fix:** a span start that is no date or period is a component carrying that refusal, at the row's or the rule's line. The order meets the refusal only where refcore would crash, comparing it with a day; elsewhere the reprint is refcore's (one such row alone reprints, as in refcore).
- **Test:** `tests/format_cases.rs`, `a_span_start_that_is_no_date_is_refused_as_load_refuses_it`. The input can go to `fuzz/regressions/format/` (a rules step).
- **Totals:** `milestone-report: M0: 34/115 green`. Exception delta +0. No STOP-REPORT.

## 2026-11-04 M0, G3: the namespace, the headers, worlds, printed load refusals and `REQ-ASK` (issue #56)

- **Built:**
  - `load/worlds.rs`, refcore's `_run_texts` and `run_worlds` up to its asks:
    - every file is split and its scenario header read, in full-path order, before any is parsed (`scenario ID over ID`, else `LOAD-PARSE`);
    - a joint model (a `use` or `same` line in a model header) stops "not built" until G9;
    - a reality with scenario files loads as worlds: the base alone (its refusal is the run's, renamed `LOAD-SCENARIO <x> of scenario <s> is read outside it` where refcore renames it), then each scenario by id, with the base's files and its stack from the model up;
    - a stack that names no world, cycles, is deeper than 4, or holds a scenario declared twice refuses that scenario alone, at its header;
    - a refused scenario's file still declares its asks.
  - `model/world.rs`: `Scenarios`, each loaded scenario's `over` and the sections its file holds. `load/steps.rs` builds it in pass 1, with refcore's `scenario … declared twice` and `is over …, neither the model nor a scenario`; the old "scenarios not built" stop is gone.
  - `load/names.rs`: the one namespace moved out of `steps.rs` (the built-in ids, `parties` among them), plus refcore's `band <b> has a built-in's name` (`LOAD-DUP`), which S lacked.
  - `ask/worlds.rs`: the asks of a reality with scenarios. Each refused scenario prints `scenario <s>: refused: …` first. The asks go by id then world, the base's first. An ask whose `in`, diff worlds, `{scenario s}` pin or own file names a refused scenario prints `<q>: refused: …`. An ask in the base runs on S's evaluator. An ask evaluated in a scenario's world stops the run "not built" (M1's): that is the per-ask stop. The worlds' warnings are merged as refcore's `unread_everywhere` merges them.
  - `ask/answer.rs`: `REQ-ASK no ask <q>` (E §11, 2.1.3) for the first unknown id, checked before any ask is answered, in plain realities and worlds alike (was `LOAD-NAME no ask`).
  - `api.rs`: a load refusal prints (was "not implemented"); `tally::load_report(Vec<Given>)`, load alone (its one refusal, or each refused scenario's line and the warnings), the entry point for the `load` fuzz target, ready for a rules step.
  - `syntax`: `parse_split` parses already-split files, so a plain run splits each file once.
- **Deviation from the plan:** the symbol table is still keyed by name (`Reality.names`), the sort its value. The (path, …) part of BUILD-ORDER's key is a member's prefix, which exists only once joint models load, so it lands at G9 with them rather than as an always-empty field now.
- **Green ids (23 new):**
  - R1: `m0_load_refusal_law_13_rate25`, `_rand_04_plan_b`, `_rule_09_inpath`, `_scen_01_amend`, `_scen_02_kept`, `_scen_03_infl8`, `_scen_04_crash`, `_surf_04_redefine`. Also `_rule_04` (S's strata already meet its `LOAD-CYCLE`; now it prints).
  - `m0_load_depth_a_ties_by_term_counts_one_level` (its `LOAD-DUP` control).
  - Per-code: `m0_code_load_header`, `_version`, `_dup`, `_reserved`. Beyond the plan, nine whose refusals S's loader already met and now print: `_ambig`, `_gaprole`, `_governs`, `_instant`, `_kind`, `_known`, `_leaf`, `_lifespan`, `_placekind`.
- **Not green, and why:**
  - `m0_load_order_the_checks_past_parsing`: `order/passes` is green; its folder's other three fixtures are G4b's (`LOAD-KEY`, `LOAD-LITERAL`).
  - The row-2 load groups: 262 vectors stop "not implemented" (forms S's loader doesn't build). The rest print the probe's `REQ-ASK` where a later group's check refuses. One fixture with two faults, `basic/key-id`, prints S's row check before refcore's `check_key_ids` (G5), as plan §4 foresaw. No `ENGINE-INVARIANT`, no panic.
- **Own tests (not gating):** `load/worlds.rs` (header before split order, a scenario alone, stacks, cycles, a scenario declared twice, ids and asks read from text); `load/names.rs` (a second id, `parties`, a band named `bal`); `api.rs` (a printed load refusal, `REQ-ASK`, a run as worlds: a base ask answered, a scenario ask not built, a refused scenario's line).
