- **#86 (L §3.1 `key K`, §5.7):** `load/observed.rs` `mv_ref_kind`: a move's instance an observed attribute names is of the kind its move declares (`MoveInfo.kind`, refcore's `move_kind`), taken where that kind is in the attribute's kind's subtree, else `LOAD-KEY <instance> is not a key of <kind>` at the observation's line, as an observed row is (the "not built" stop is gone). At run the attribute is the movement it names, found by its id at the reader's cut (refcore's `_obs_attr`, an `mvref`): `obs_fields` keeps it among the observation's values as `MvRef { src: Missing }` (`book/members.rs` `mv_record` already finds such a value), and `ask/answer.rs` `windows_or_moves` no longer stops an ask on it. Probes (6): the attribute printed (`g (r = stmt[r1]): due[2026-03]`), its record read (`r.of_due.cash.amount`), `r.of_due in dues`, `r.of_due == r`; an instance of a key outside the move's index (`due[2027-03]`, `due[2025-03]`) differs, refcore's text wrong (the STOP-REPORT below).
- **P29 (L §6.4, §8.3 item 9; 2.1.6-25):** `load/across.rs`: `existence_seen` (no `when`, and each binding's `where` reads nothing but keys: `seen` with `Seen::Where`, refcore's `cond`, which also takes a comparison, `and`, `or`, `not` and a truth literal); `check_scenario_moves` folds only a move whose day load sees and whose existence it sees, and skips a choice of keys whose `where` folds false or is refused (`no_instance`), refcore's order. The walk leaves out an instance of a scenario's move whose day or a leg's own day falls before the scenario's `from` (`walk/plan.rs` `status`, new `scenario_early`, `scenario_from`; refcore's `scen_early`), and a decision's instance dated before it (`walk/agenda.rs` `schedule_decision`, refcore's `walk.py:521`), each counted `left out`. Probes (12): the clean fixture, a `where` over a declared year index true or false before the from, a `where` reading an input, a declared day index before the from, a leg's own day before the from with and without the `when`, a decision with and without an early `when`; refcore's bytes in each (rules refuse a literal, so the probes use declared indexes).
- **C1, P8 (L §6.4, §5.18, E §11; #73):** an ask evaluated in one loaded scenario's world is answered on that scenario's reality (`Worlds.loaded`: the base's files and its stack), with its own engines, memo and booked amounts (refcore's one `Asker` per world): `ask/answer.rs` `Turn::Ask(world, at)`, `answer_turns` over the run's realities (the base's first, then each loaded scenario's by id), a `World` per reality; the warnings are the realities' merged, then each world's walks' own, the base's first, each once (refcore's `run_worlds`). `ask/worlds.rs` `world_turn`: no world but the model's, the base's ask; one loaded scenario's, its reality's ask of that id. `ask_body` takes an ask `in` the reality's own world (`model/world.rs` `Scenarios::own`, the top of its stack) and stops `NotBuilt` for any other: an ask evaluated in two worlds, a difference, and a scenario file's ask `in` the model (which hides the scenario's layer, refcore `200.00 PLN` where the whole stack gives 300) stay M1's overlays. Probes (5): every ask of the `scen` fixture, two scenarios over one base with their own asks and a scenario's unread input, named in another order.

The gating log's red ids are the `Logged-Red:` ids of G2, G3 and G4a (`g1b/ids-1.txt` against G1a's `g1a/red-2.txt`: four fewer, none new); `check fast`: "FAIL gating: S, M0 gate, and 28 of their 155 tests are red" (30 at the start).

## 2026-11-07 pre-M1, G2: the walk: the typed order of a day's refused plans, refused movements reach as holes do, a refused leg's re-measurement (issues #97, #57, #51, #52)

The builder of the pre-M1 run, group G2 of `tmp/pre-m1-build/plan.md` (the coordinator's P11, P13), on G1b's fbe9d6e: the seven `Logged-Red:` ids T marked G2. Each vector was reproduced first on the debug binary (`tmp/pre-m1-build/g2/kv.py`, the kernel store), and the edges probed on refcore and the engine (`g2/kp.py`; `p1.py` to `p3.py`, 15 probes).

- **#57 (E §4.1, 2.1.6-4):** a day's plan legs are ranked by the typed movement order, each term as its type (refcore's `plan_key`), for every status, a refused instance's too: `Walk::plan_rank` over `Walk.orders`, the walk's plan and decision instances' orders sorted (`movement_orders`); a modelled-past instance's `open` mark and a decision's leg on its own later day take the same rank (refcore's `plan_key` at both). 2.1.5's text rank is gone: `Walk::text_rank`, `Walk.order_texts`, `Order::text` and `quoted` are deleted. **Two of B6's six `R-87: missed:` rows name deleted functions** (`walk/plan.rs` `Order::text`, both; `walk/state.rs` `Walk::text_rank`, both), and `schedule_plan`'s `replace == with !=` (l.425) names a comparison that is gone (`inst.status == Status::Refused` chose the move's text); its `delete field rank from struct At` (the `point` closure) is still there: the batch after G4a (P13) judges it, since a day's plans are also put on the agenda in that same typed order, so the rank may only matter where a mark or a decision's later leg meets them. The rows stay in `engine/MUTANTS-SURVIVORS.md` for that batch, as the brief says.
- **#51, plans (L §8.4, §5.14 Keys; E §4.1 step 2, §4.2; 2.1.6-2):** a member whose key's `where` is refused at run is kept with its refusal (`eval/aggregate.rs` `binding_members`, `where_keeps`, `Kept`), and its tuple ends there (`walk/plan.rs` `Tuple.refused`, `move_tuples`); `refused_tuple` makes it a refused instance, its day and body read as any instance's, none where its `when` is false or its keys weren't there; a tuple whose later keys are unbound refuses the move's instances, every place from the run's start (refcore's `insts_of_tuples`). A refused instance whose evaluation stopped before its legs (its day, `when`, an attribute or `for`) has its legs' places read at its booking cut and law date, the refusal as each leg's amount, none where a place isn't a thing (`eval_inst`, `refused_legs`, `refused_places`, `read_or_refused`; refcore's `refused_places`); an instance refused before its day has its legs dated the end of time, no day, so no scenario's `from` leaves it out (refcore's legs there carry no day). The walk's refused arm was already refcore's `sched_refused`: from its day the legs' places, every place where one can't be read. `link_tuple` takes a refused tuple as existing. Probes (9, all refcore's bytes): a `where` refused at a binding that isn't the last, with a false `when`, with its leg's place refused, with its day refused; a refused `when` with its place refused, with a leg on its own day; an attribute refused on a move of two legs.
- **#51, decisions (L §8.4, §6.4; E §4.2):** a decision instance refused before its point (its key's `where`) and dated in the walk refuses from its day the places its legs give, read at the walk's cut, each in every unit, or every place where its move has legs whose places can't be read (`walk/agenda.rs` `refused_decisions`, `refused_day`, `decision_refused`; refcore's `dec_bad`), and `flows` of the move over a span it may fall in is refused, as is one whose day is refused (`walk/reads.rs` `decision_runs`). `Act::ChangeRefused` is now `Act::PlaceBad` (refcore's `placebad`), shared by an adjustment's refused change. **The order G1b's review asked about:** the engine checks the scenario's `from` first (L §6.4: "An instance whose existence load can't decide is left out at run if its day falls before D"), as `status` does for a plan, where refcore's `prepare` checks `dec_bad` first (walk.py:514-519); the spec decides it, and refcore's branch never fires anyway (the STOP-REPORT below). L §6.4 is on `schedule_decision`'s doc comment now. These four probes differ from refcore, by the STOP-REPORT.
- **#52 (E §5 step 3, L §5.14.3, §8.4; 2.1.6-3):** at a step whose `when` is a hole, and at a step measured against a hole state, a leg whose amount is refused on either side has its δ refused, the other legs' stay holes (`book/adjust.rs` `hole_when`, `leg_refusal`, `against_hole`; refcore's `leg_refusal`, the state measured against counted where it existed). Probes (2, refcore's bytes): a refused cash leg at the step after a hole `when`, and a hole `when` after a step that refused it.
- **Green ids (7):** `g6/refusal_order`, `g6/refusal_keys_typed`, `g6/when`, `g6/where_refused`, `g7/flows`, `g11/remeasure_refused_leg_hole_when`, `g14/move_day_row`. ## STOP-REPORT 2026-11-07: refcore drops a decision instance whose key's `where` is refused, where L §8.4 makes it a refused movement

- **Kind:** other (refcore differs from a clear spec; the engine follows the spec; no vector holds it)
- **Where:** `engine/tools/refcore/moves.py` `m_decision_instances` (:1143): a tuple whose status is `('refused', e)` takes the domain-hole branch (`inst.day = st[1]`, `inst.exists = st[1]`: the refusal as its day, `err` none), so `walk.py` `prepare`'s #51 branch (:514, `dec_bad`) never fires and the instance books, refuses and lists nothing; `mreads.py` `m_flows_move` (:1480) counts an unrun decision instance only for a hole day. The engine's `walk/plan.rs` `refused_tuple`, `walk/agenda.rs` `refused_decisions`, `decision_refused`, `walk/reads.rs` `decision_runs`.
- **Rule or gate:** L §8.4 (a movement refused by its key's `where` reaches as a hole movement does: its day and its legs' places are still read; from its day, every day for a refused day, it refuses the places its legs give and every `flows` over a span it may fall in; a leg whose place can't be read refuses every place); L §5.14 Keys; E §4.2 Holes in the walk; CHANGES 2.1.6-2 (#51).
- **What happens:** on `kernel/g5/refused.tally` with `move split[m in month where 1 / divisor[m] > 0]` and its `when` line taken out, `tally run refused.tally` prints `q_bal (d = 2026-02-20): (100.00 PLN, refused: RUN-DIV division by zero)` and the same at 2026-03-20 (box, which `split[2026-02]` moves into, refused from its day; konto stands); refcore prints `(100.00 PLN, 3.00 PLN)` and `(100.00 PLN, 4.00 PLN)`, `split[2026-02]` gone. With its leg's place refused at that key (`to (if 1 / divisor[m] > 0 then box else konto)`) the engine refuses both places from 2026-02-20; refcore as before. `flows(split, 2026-02-01 .. 2026-02-28)`: the engine `refused: RUN-DIV division by zero`, refcore `0.00 PLN`; the same for a decision whose day is refused at that key (`on day_of(m, dec_dom * divisor[m] / divisor[m])`): the engine refused, refcore `0.00 PLN` (its balances agree: every place refused from the start). With a binding after the refused one (`n in month`) the engine refuses every place from the run's start, as for a plan's instances (refcore's `insts_of_tuples` raises), where refcore runs the tuples whose later key is none and prints `RUN-TIE split has a tie at split[2026-01, 2026-01], …`. Probes `tmp/pre-m1-build/g2/p1.py` (`dec-where`, `dec-where-place`), `p2.py` (`dec-where-flows`, `dec-where-not-last`), `p3.py` (`dec-day-flows`).
- **Also, the order of the two rules:** a decision instance refused before its point and dated before the `from` of the scenario that declares it is left out (L §6.4: "An instance whose existence load can't decide is left out at run if its day falls before D"), the scenario checked first as `status` checks it for a plan; refcore's `prepare` checks `dec_bad` first (walk.py:514-519), which by the above never fires.
- **What I tried:** nothing to try: the spec is clear, and refcore's own comment at walk.py:515 states the engine's reading.
- **What I ask:** refcore's `decision_instances` makes a refused tuple a refused instance at its keys (its `err` set, as `insts_of_tuples` does for a plan, raising where a later key is unbound), and `flows_move` counts an unrun decision instance whose day or key is refused as its refusal; then T adds vectors of these forms at the next round (none today: no vector reaches a refused decision).
- **State:** G2's commit holds this report; no test, spec or refcore file touched.

## 2026-11-07 pre-M1, G4a: #90's probes, the stops no text reaches, the reviews' list (issues #97, #90, #100)

The builder of the pre-M1 run, group G4a of `tmp/pre-m1-build/plan.md` (the coordinator's P1, P2, P7, P11, P13), on G3's 5815c57: the eight `Logged-Red:` ids T marked G4a (0b4beea, bbe4601; `g14/day_range_end`, the ninth, green since G1a's review), the three held for #100 built to P1 and P2's form, #90's stops no vector reaches (plan §1.2, §1.4), and the reviews' items the plan marks G4a (§1.3). Each vector was reproduced first on the debug binary (`tmp/pre-m1-build/g4a/kv.py`, `fx.py`), and its edges probed on refcore and the engine (`g4a/pz.py`, 39 probes, `pz-final.out`).

## STOP-REPORT 2026-11-07: refcore checks a stepped range's written keys at the first ask, so a reality no ask reads loads, and `REQ-ASK` outranks the refusal

- **Kind:** other (refcore differs from a clear spec; the engine follows the spec; no vector holds it)
- **Where:** `engine/tools/refcore/evaluate.py` `check_range_lits`, called from `ask.py` `_answer` at a reality's first ask; the engine's `load/keys.rs` `check_range_keys`, once load is done (`load/steps.rs` `load_among`).
- **Rule or gate:** L §2.5 Keys ("Load refuses what it can see …: a literal key where the range's ends and step are literals is `LOAD-KEY`"); E §11 `REQ-ASK` ("after load (a load refusal outranks it)").
- **What happens:** on `codes/m0/written/base.tally` with `rr[3]` written `rr[4]` and its one ask taken out, `tally run a.tally` exits 1 with `refused: LOAD-KEY 4 is not a value of 1 .. 9 step 2 (a.tally:41)`; refcore loads clean (exit 0, its warnings only). With the ask kept and `--ask nope`, the engine prints the same `LOAD-KEY`; refcore `refused: REQ-ASK no ask nope`. Probes `tmp/pre-m1-build/g4a/pz.py` (`rk-4-noask`, `rk-4-reqask`). A run that answers an ask of the base prints the same bytes in both (`written/range-key-frac`; `rk-4`, `rk-4-ask`).
- **What I tried:** nothing to try: the spec is clear.
- **What I ask:** refcore checks the written keys as its last load check, before `REQ-ASK`, at the next round; T then adds vectors of the two forms.
- **State:** G4a's commit holds this report; no test, spec or refcore file touched.

## 2026-11-07 pre-M1, G4b (1 of 2): one interner of names, and tables a typed id finds (issues #97, #90)

The builder of the pre-M1 run, group G4b of `tmp/pre-m1-build/plan.md` § "G4b, typed ids" (BUILDER §3, D2-3, D2-4; the coordinator's P10, P11), its commit (1), on 2fdf539. Structural: no vector, no output changes.

- **The interner (`base/symbol.rs`, new):** `Symbol(u32)`, with no `Ord` and no `Hash` (R-47: an id never orders anything; an order of names is their texts'); `Interner` (`intern`, `find`, `text`), past 2^32 texts `Internal`. `Internal`, a broken invariant of the engine's tables, becomes `ENGINE-INVARIANT unreachable: <what should have held>` through `Stop`'s `From` (`syntax/parse.rs`, beside `From<Refusal>`).
- **`Table<I, T>` (`base/table.rs`):** today's table, keyed by an id type too. Its text lookups stay (`get`, `get_mut`, `contains`, `entries`, `names`, insertion order; P10's C3 (a)); `at(id)` and `at_mut(id)` return `Result<_, Internal>` through a dense slot vector by the name's symbol, never a map keyed by an id; still no `Index` impl. `push` takes the reality's interner and interns the name itself, so an item's symbol is its name's by construction.
- **The ids and the namespace (`model/ids.rs`, new):** twelve ids (`SourceId`, `VersionId`, `KindId`, `ThingId`, `IndexId`, `InputId`, `DefId`, `BandId`, `PlaceId`, `MoveId`, `AxisId`, `FillId`), each a `Symbol` made only in `model/` and turned back by `From<…> for Symbol`; `Name`, a name's sort with its id (`Name::kind()` gives `NameKind`); `Names`, the one namespace (`declare` through the interner; `get`, `kind`, `contains_key`, `entries`, text order). The reality holds `symbols: Interner` and `names: Names`, and its twelve tables are typed.
- **Callers:** the 65 namespace lookups that compare a sort read `names.kind(id)` (was `names.get(id)`, one call each, the `&` patterns gone); the 19 pushes pass `&mut self.m.symbols` and `?`; `register_builtins` returns a `Result`.
- **Ids turned green:** none (G4b has no vector). check full: "check full: green, 31 steps (1335s)" (guard-test skipped as its inputs are a green run's); gating and gating-release "155 tests run: 155 passed, 0 skipped", no gating id red.
- **Kernel files:** none touched; no mutants-local run.
- **Size:** 35 files, +678 −149 in engine/crates (`model/ids.rs` 294, `base/symbol.rs` 119, `base/table.rs` +109 −22, own tests 84 of the lines; the rest one-for-one at the callers). The plan's whole group is +450 −200, so this commit alone is the brief's 1.5×: I stopped here, green, as the brief says.
- **Exception delta:** count +0 (6); report-only hits 5 → 8, the three `type-parameter-review` hits of `Table<T>` now six (its struct, its `Default` and its methods each take `I` and `T`, R-124's expected hit), `engine/tools/exceptions --write` in this commit.
- **Deviations from the plan (each the recommended form, logged):** (1) `push(symbols, name, item)`, not `push(id, name, item)`: the table interns the name, so no caller can pass an id of another text, and the 19 pushes need no id from the namespace. (2) The ids reach their symbol by std's `From`, not an own trait (R-124: no new trait). (3) The namespace is a type of its own, `Names`, with `kind(id)`, so each of the 65 sites changes one call rather than gaining `.map(Name::kind)`. (4) `Symbol` has no `Ord`, where BUILDER §3's letter orders it by its text; the brief's rule (R-47) wins, and nothing needs the order yet.

## 2026-11-08 M1, G1: the written year forms, #109's key order, a scenario's replace on its own day, a bound name's field (issue #108)

The builder of the M1 run, group G1 of `tmp/m1/build-plan.md` (picks P3, P6, P12, P15, P23), on T's 91e433b. Each id was reproduced first on the debug binary, and its edges probed on refcore and the engine (`tmp/m1/g1/probe.py`, `specs1.txt` to `specs4.txt`, `years/`, `field/`, `narrow/`).

- **The written year forms (P6, P12; L §2.1):** load reads a four-digit figure as a year where a period is read, and the run now learns the reading the same way it learns the rest: from the expression as written and the place it stands in. `eval/years.rs` (new): `year_form` (a four-digit figure, or an `if` with one among its arms, through brackets), `as_year`, `span_year`, `year_literal`. A side of a comparison in a year form beside a period is that year (`year_of(d) == 2026`); the first operand of a sum before a duration or a period is a year (`2026 + 1 year`, the one reading load's `ambig` lets through); an int at a span's end is a year (a figure, or a key-list variable of four-digit keys, refcore's `kl_value(e, 'year')`); a period argument in a year form is that year (replaces `eval/builtin.rs` `written_year`); a key in a year form gives the key literal of its digits, which its position resolves as it resolves the bare figure. Load: an `if` at a key of a year index whose arms hold a four-digit figure is typed with its arms as years (`load/keyed.rs` `key_pos`; the ruling on #113: an `if`'s arms take the type of the place it stands in).
- **A key list fixed by its key positions (L §2.5):** refcore resolves a key list's literals as keys of the index its key positions fix (`b._var.fixed`); the engine kept them ints, so `rate[y] for y in [2026, 2027]` was `RUN-KEY 2 026 is outside ys` (the six `pit37` ids). A binding now carries its `Site` (file, line, token; `syntax/tree.rs`, no part of a tree's equality); the checker's `fix_list` records each list's site with the index it fixed (`Checker.list_keys`, through `Walked` to `Reality.list_keys`), and `bind_values` resolves the list's literals as that index's keys. An unfixed list keeps its ints (`y + 1 for y in [2026, 2027]` is `2 027`, as refcore).
- **Parenthesised literals (P12; L §2.1):** a literal position takes `(lit)` (`syntax/parse.rs` `paren_lit`, from `lit` and `lit_unsigned`: lists, `governs`, row keys), its brackets marked plain so the canonical form drops them; in an expression the tree keeps the bracket, so a message prints it as written (`LOAD-AMBIG (2027) + 1 reads as …`, as refcore), and `Expr::unbracketed` lets a key position (load's `key_pos`, the run's `given_key`) and the year forms see through it.
- **#109, the canonical order of keys of different kinds (P3; L §2.4):** a key in text order (an open index, a domain the part doesn't declare) sorts by its literal's kind first, in the grammar's order (a number, a date, a month-day, a key, a duration, `true` / `false`), then a number or a duration by value, a date by its first day then its text, any other by its text (`syntax/canon_order.rs` `kind_first`); a place keyed by things sorts with the keys. `key_kinds.tally` reprints as itself; no single-kind text moved (m0_format, s_* green).
- **A scenario's `replace` on its own known day (L §6.4, §4.6 step 2):** a scenario's replacement beats its base's row or entry whatever their known dates: `model/world.rs` `Scenarios::layer` (a bounded walk up the `over` chain; `load/overlap.rs`'s `layer` calls it); `load/kinds.rs` lets a replace stand when an entry it replaces is known earlier or lies on a layer below; `read/rows.rs` `unreplaced` (kernel): a row is beaten by a replacement on a higher layer, or on its own layer known later. Step 3.1 (the top layer wins) stays G6's.
- **A bound name's field (#90, pick P20 of pre-M1; L §5.7, §8.3):** load keeps a kind test's narrowing (`Scope.narrow`, `Scope::narrowed`, refcore's `narrow_tests` and `narrowed`): a test `v in K` with no `on`, alone or a conjunct, narrows `v` to a strict subkind in a rule's `when` and value (`load/defs.rs`) and in an `if`'s condition and `then` branch (`load/ops.rs`); `movement_field` reads the subkind's legs and attributes there (`kind_field`, split out), and a field of a name no test narrows is `LOAD-FIELD`, where it was `Unseen`. Probes against refcore (`narrow/a1`–`a6`): narrowing in an `if`, none in a `where` or an `else`, none through `not` or `or`, all equal.

## 2026-11-08 M1, G2: the date, calendar and number built-ins, windows read where a day is needed (issue #108)

The builder of the M1 run, group G2 of `tmp/m1/build-plan.md` (picks P15, P16, P12, P23), on b8bd1d9. Each red id was reproduced first on the debug binary; the edges were probed on refcore and the engine (`tmp/m1/g2/edges/`, 96 asks over windows, `one_of`, ages, `add_years`, `overlap_days`, `dcf`, `pow`, `last_business`, `add_business`, `is_business`, `schedule`, `min`/`max`, `round`, an `if` over a window): all 96 equal, byte for byte.

- **Windows at run (L §3.1, §4.4, §8.1):** a row's `window(a, b)` or `one_of([…])` is a value (`model/value.rs` `Value::Window`, `Window { lo, hi, days, src, origin }`), its origin the row it was read from (`eval/read.rs`). `eval/windows.rs` (new) is refcore's `Iv`: arithmetic on its ends (two windows give `HOLE-IMPRECISE`); a comparison decided where the whole interval lies on one side, a window against itself decided (`==`, `<=`, `>=` true), a `one_of`'s `==`/`!=` by its days; `window(a, b)` and `one_of(…)` called in a rule (`RUN-DOMAIN window(a, b) with a after b`, `one_of of no days`); `one_day` (refcore's `date_of`) for a use that needs one day, else the hole `date within <w> not precise enough for <use>`, its figure the read's. A print of a window of days is `window(a, b)` or `one_of([…])`, any other interval the hole `… for a print` (`ask/answer.rs`). A list's names are read as names (`eval/expr.rs` `list`, refcore's `e_list`: `one_of([d, 2026-03-01])`), as load typed them.
- **The date built-ins (L §4.1, §4.2, §3.3):** `eval/dates.rs` (new, split from `builtin.rs`): `start_of`, `end_of`, `year_of`, `month_of`, `quarter_of`, `day_of` (moved), `days_between`, `add_days`, `add_months`, `add_years` (×12, `LOAD-UNIT a duration of <u> for add_years`), `age`, `age_months`, `overlap_days`, `weekday` (a window's weekdays, 1..7 at seven days or more), `dcf(a, b, act365)` (days / 365, negative when b is before a; another code `LOAD-UNIT dcf: <w> is not a daycount code`), each lifted over a window as refcore's `lift` (equal ends one value; two windows, or `none` at an end, the hole).
- **The calendar built-ins (L §4.2, §4.4):** `time/calendar.rs` keeps the calendar's rules (`weekends`, `yearly … from Y`, `added` as `Added::{Day, Hole, Window, OneOf}`); `eval/business.rs` (new) holds `is_business`, `add_business` (`RUN-ZERO`), `last_business` (`RUN-DOMAIN last_business with n = <n> below 1`, `… the period has fewer business days`), `schedule` and its rolls, each run as refcore's `over_added`: passes from a worklist, a pass that meets an added window no choice names run again once per day of it, the holes merged, equal results that value, `is_business` results that differ `HOLE-IMPRECISE`, other results the interval `window(min, max)`.
- **The number built-ins (L §3.3, §7.1):** `pow(x, k)` by an integer (refcore's `c_pow`: a `none` refused before a hole spreads; a non-integer power `NotBuilt`, the transcendental kernel's; the digits screen `RUN-DIGITS pow(x, k) passes 4 096 digits; round where the law rounds`; `RUN-DIV division by zero: pow(0, k)`; unit^k), through the kernel's `Num::checked_pow` (repeated squaring, checked) and `Num::bits`; a rate's `power` uses `checked_pow`. `round` of a window is the hole `… for round`; `min`/`max` over windows by their ends (`extreme_of`).
- **G1's review note 11:** `round(wage[2026-06] * rate[2026], 0.01, half-even)` was refused "different units"; a bare step written with no unit and not a fraction takes the operand's unit (check.py `_stepflex`; `eval/builtin.rs` `bare_step`): `500.00 PLN`, as refcore.
- **Green ids (11):** m1_checks `U:time/TIME-01`, `TIME-02`, `TIME-04`, `TIME-05`, `U:num/NUM-01`, `NUM-02`, `U:life/LIFE-03` (the test `m1_checks_g2`); m1_carried item 6, N7's four of G2: `carried/steps/n7_weekday` (`(1, 7)`), `n7_last_business` (`(2026-06-30, 2026-05-28)` with its assumed line), an `added` window both ways, `n7_window_agrees` (`2026-06-04`) and `n7_window_differs` (`window(2026-06-24, 2026-06-25)`). The report: `milestone-report: M1: 23/43 green` at b8bd1d9, `milestone-report: M1: 24/43 green` now (m1_checks_g2; item 6's test waits on N1, N4, B1 and N7's `explain`, G13's). Of the plan's 16: `RUN-ZERO` was green before M1 and stays green; the two `RUN-DOMAIN` forms (`<operation> has no meaning for <values>`, `<code> is not a <family> code`) have no vector, and refcore prints neither on G2's built-ins (its texts, which the engine prints: `window(a, b) with a after b`, `one_of of no days`, `last_business with n = <n> below 1`, `… fewer business days`).
- **Size:** 18 files, +1 915 −591 in engine/crates, about 280 lines of them moved (the date built-ins, `day_of`, `period_arg`, `months_arg`, the old calendar walk): about +1 630 −310 beyond the moves (plan +450 −60, expected +720 −100): 3.6× the plan, past the brief's 1.8×, reported; 2.3× the expected. The most: `eval/business.rs` 512 (new), `eval/dates.rs` 490 (new), `eval/windows.rs` 331 (new), `eval/builtin.rs` +193 −441, `time/calendar.rs` +111 −102, `num/rational.rs` +77, `model/value.rs` +65, `eval/convert.rs` +56 −24, `eval/expr.rs` +44 −20. Over the plan: the plan sized the built-ins over single days; a window read where a day is needed makes every date built-in a lift over an interval (refcore's `Iv`, `lift`, `iv_compare`, `iv_arith`), and a calendar's added window makes every business-day function a set of passes (`over_added`), neither in the plan's count.
- **Files (for the map, P19):** new `eval/windows.rs`: a day not yet known at run, its comparisons, arithmetic and the hole a use that needs one day makes; new `eval/dates.rs`: the date built-ins, each over a window too (split from `builtin.rs`); new `eval/business.rs`: the calendar built-ins over every day an added window takes (split from `builtin.rs` and `time/calendar.rs`); `eval/builtin.rs` now the dispatch and the number built-ins. Changed: `lib.rs`, `ask/answer.rs`; `eval/` `arith.rs`, `convert.rs`, `expr.rs`, `read.rs`, `state.rs`; `load/` `bands.rs`, `texts.rs`; `model/value.rs`; `num/rational.rs`; `time/calendar.rs`; `walk/` `plan.rs`, `reads.rs` (a window where a movement's day, a unit's name or a band's end is read: `NotBuilt`, no vector reaches them).
- **Kernel functions touched:** `num/rational.rs` `Num::checked_pow`, `Num::bits` (L §3.3, §7.1). `time/**` stays outside the kernel (P16).
- **mutants-local** (`--next -F 'checked_pow|bits'`, `num/rational.rs`): 17 mutants, 7 caught, 9 missed, 1 unviable (12 min). For T, each told apart by an ask on a vector (my `a_whole_power_is_exact` kills each, but the lib's own tests aren't in the run): `rational.rs:162` the zero guard `self.is_zero() && k < 0`, `&&` → `||` (`pow(1.5, -2)` is `4/9`), `<` → `==` and `<=` (`pow(0, 0)` is `1`), `<` → `>` (`pow(0, 2)` is `0`); `:173` `left > 0` → `>=`, one more square after the last bit (`pow(10, 3000)` passes the screen and is exact; the extra square, 10^4096, passes the cap and refuses); `:177` `k < 0` → `==` (`pow(1.5, -2)`); `:186` `Num::bits` → `0` and → `1` (the screen never refuses: `pow(10, 5000)` stops at the cap inside the power, not at the screen's `RUN-DIGITS pow(x, k) passes 4 096 digits; round where the law rounds`, refcore's text). Equivalent: `:177` `k < 0` → `<=` (at `k = 0` the power is 1, and `1 / 1` is 1); no row, since its key, `replace < with <= in Num::checked_pow`, also names `:162`'s mutant, which a test can tell apart. The guard at `:162` is redundant (`1 / 0^|k|` is already `RUN-DIV` through `checked_div`); removing it would leave the key to the equivalent one alone, for the review to judge.
- **Own tests (not gating):** `num/rational.rs` `a_whole_power_is_exact`, `bits_are_the_larger_part_s`; `time/calendar.rs` `weekends_and_yearly_days_are_off_by_rule`, `an_added_window_names_its_days`; `eval/windows.rs` `a_one_of_holds_only_its_days`.

## 2026-11-08 M1, G3: the extremes with ties by, marginal, last(x, d), pos(k ± n), a movement as a def's key (issue #108)

The builder of the M1 run, group G3 of `tmp/m1/build-plan.md` (pick P15), on 95d5f72, beside G2's review (P26). Each red id was reproduced first on the debug binary; the edges were probed on refcore and the engine (`tmp/m1/g3/`: `ties/` 24 asks, `marg/` 14, `pos/` 8, `ask01/` 6, `pty01/` 5, `time06/` 4, `val01/` 7, `law06/` 7, `codes/` the rules family with and without its decision): all equal but one, the STOP-REPORT below.

- **The extremes (P15; L §5.11, §4.4):** `eval/extremes.rs` (new): `argmin` and `argmax` with `ties by` (one term or a tuple, the least score wins; `RUN-TIE … that changes the result, and no ties by` / `… and its ties by doesn't break it`, the tied keys listed; of windows, the key decided below (above) every other, else `HOLE-IMPRECISE`); `first` and `last` in binding order, or over things, movements and an open index's keys in the order of the `ties by` (a stable sort; `RUN-TIE first over things has no ties by` where load doesn't see the binding), a hole met before the first true key making the result that hole, the register of only the members met (`unfulfilled: pay`, not `repay`); `HOLE-NOTYET` (`m: no transfers yet, and its span reaches past the cut`) over a binding of things or movements empty at the cut whose day is after it or whose span reaches past it, else `RUN-EMPTY first over no keys`; `min` and `max` over windows in an aggregate as in a call (`min_max`, shared with `builtin.rs`'s `min(a, b)`). Refcore's twins: `agg_arg`, `tie_break`, `agg_first`, `tie_groups`, `notyet_bind`.
- **`marginal(b, x)` (L §5.9):** `eval/band.rs` `Engine::marginal`: the rows whose lower end is below x, each value with x bound times `min(x, hi) − lo`, a `num` in x's unit times the rows' (`14 400 DUK`); 0 in that unit below the first row; the holes of hole rows; `LOAD-BAND` for a first row from `-inf`; a band read's key, argument and rows shared with `b(x)` (`band_rows_at`, `BandRead`, `no_row`). A running total over earlier keys under `marginal` (TIME-06) needed nothing more.

## STOP-REPORT 2026-11-08: refcore answers an argmin's tie with the key whose ties by term is a hole; the engine answers the hole

- **Kind:** other (refcore differs from the spec; the engine follows the spec)
- **Where:** refcore `evaluate.py` `tie_break` (`for v in vals: if isinstance(v, Hole): return [(e2, v)]`, then `agg_arg` returns `self.bare_key(n, best[0][0])`, the key of that member, not its hole); the engine's `eval/extremes.rs` `Engine::tie_break` (a `ties by` score that is a hole is the result).
- **Rule or gate:** L §5.11 (the aggregates' table: `argmin`, `argmax` "a hole if any term is"; "A hole carries the questions of every operand, `where` and term it evaluated"), §8.1 (holes spread; a hole never becomes a value), P-9; DONE gate 10.
- **What happens:** on `tmp/m1/g3/ties/model.tally` (`index ks = [a, b, c, d]`, `v[b] = v[c] = 1` the least, `hv[b]` stated by no row), `tally run model.tally t3.tally --ask t3` with `ask t3: argmin(v[k] for k in ks) ties by hv[k]`: refcore `t3: b`, exit 0; the engine `t3: ?"hv at b: no value" (hv[b])`, exit 0. Which of b and c wins depends on `hv[b]`, which no row gives; refcore picks b because its tie term is the hole. The same in `first`/`last` over things (refcore's `tie_groups`) is a hole on both: refcore and the engine agree there.
- **What I tried:** nothing on refcore or the tests. The other 23 probes of `tmp/m1/g3/ties/` (`ties by` one and two terms, a tie it doesn't break, no `ties by`, over things on a day after the cut, a hole `where` before the first key, `last`, `argmax`, windows) are byte-equal on both.
- **What I ask:** a ruling that a hole in a `ties by` term among tied candidates makes the result that hole (the engine's reading), refcore's `tie_break` to return the hole at M2's round, and T to hold the form "for #n" when it writes a vector of it (none of M1's vectors reaches it).
- **State:** G3's commits carry the code; no test, spec or refcore file touched.

- 2026-11-08 G3's review (`tmp/m1/review-g3.md`), its five blocking findings fixed, with picks P33 and P34 and the review's notes 3, 7 and 12 (issue #108; the rulings P29 on #116, P35 on #116–#118 and #115), on top of G2's fix db2519c. Probes: the review's `tmp/m1/g3-review/probes/` ("before" is the review's output at 203d0b6, reproduced here on db2519c's binary), new ones in `tmp/m1/g3/fix/` (`b3/` 14 asks, `nb3/` 6).
  - **B1, a crash in `first`/`last` with `ties by` over things whose scores mix windows and dates (`eval/extremes.rs` `tie_groups`), and P33, a window in a `ties by` (L §4.4: "Only a comparison decides an interval").** The scores are sorted by a merge sort (`sorted_groups`, `merge`) whose comparisons can fail: each is `windows.rs` `decided_order` (a window below or above a value when its whole interval lies on one side, two reads alike of one item one value, else undecided), an undecided one the window's `HOLE-IMPRECISE` (`tie_order`: `date within … not precise enough for the ties by of first`), so no comparison is assumed and std's sort, which panicked on a comparator answering `Equal` for a refusal, is gone. The later candidate is compared with the earlier, as refcore's sort compares them (`ties/` t16's `RUN-DOMAIN an order on things (cid, bob)` stays equal). `p8_n8` to `p8_n30` and `p8_things_mixed`: `tally run: not implemented` (exit 69) at 20 members and fewer, a panic (exit 101) from 21 → `?"date within window(…) not precise enough for the ties by of first" (w[…])` at every count; `p6_sort_windows` a, `p7_things_windows` the same hole (`… for the ties by of argmin`); refcore `refused: LOAD-UNIT … compared` on each (P33: refcore's; T holds the hole "for #115").
  - **B2, `ties by (e1, e2)` read e2 of candidates e1 had decided (L §5.11: "compares e1, then e2 among those still tied"; #116).** `score` and `tie_groups` are replaced by one order built lazily (`TieOrder`, `Engine::next_tied`, `tie_terms`): all candidates in one group, a group ordered by the next term only while it holds more than one, its subgroups pushed so the next to visit is on top (from the end for `last`); `tie_break` (argmin's) takes the first group, `first_by_ties` visits them in order. A term that is a hole for a candidate still tied is the result's hole (P29), and `none` is refused before a hole (L §8.4). `p2b_ties_e1` `?"sub at q1: no value" (sub[q1])` → `q2` (refcore `q1`, #116); `p2_ties_e2` the hole → `q1` (= refcore); `p2c_untied_hole` `q2`, unchanged; new `b3/` t3 `first(p in persons on 2026-02-01) ties by (rank[p], sub[p])` `ana` and t4 (`last`) `bob` with `sub[ana]` unread (refcore the hole of `sub[ana]`), t5 `cid`, t6 `bob` (= refcore).
  - **B3, `first`/`last` read every member's `where` (L §5.11: "each stopping at the first key whose `where` is true; those keys are what it read"; #117).** `eval/aggregate.rs`: `members`' walk is a step at a time (`Walk`, `Step`, `tuple_walk`, `next_tuple`; `members` loops over it, unchanged), each binding's values taken from the last for `last` (`Way`, `Frame::taken`); `first_in_order` stops at the first tuple its `where`s keep that is no unknown member, its movements' registers brought to the reader as met. Over things, movements or an open index's keys (`first_by_ties`), the candidates are bound with no `where` read (`bind_candidates`), ordered by the `ties by` (read for every candidate, as the order needs it), and their `where`s read group by group (`candidate_kept`) up to the first group with a true one; over a tuple of bindings with no order it stops `NotBuilt` (as `bare_key` did after it). `p1_first_stops` `refused: RUN-DIV division by zero` → `1` (refcore the refusal, #117); `b3/` l1 (`last(k in ks where k == 4 or base / (k - 3) > 0)`) `4`, t1 `bob`, t2 `ana` past members whose `where` divides by 0 (refcore `RUN-DIV` each); l2–l7 and t7 (holes before the key, a hole with no key after, `RUN-EMPTY`, `count`) equal to refcore; G3's `codes/` `nodec*` f2, f4, f5 (`pay`, `repay`, `HOLE-NOTYET`, `RUN-EMPTY`, the `assumed` lines) equal, as before.

## 2026-11-08 M1, G4: prorata's three rests on the one split, a list index's keys found by halving (issue #108)

The builder of the M1 run, group G4 of `tmp/m1/build-plan.md` (picks P15, P17, P25, P26, P37), on 6dfb2cd. Each red id was reproduced first on the debug binary (NUM-05, the `rules` family's base, `rules/weights` and `rules/prorata` all `tally run: not implemented`; B1's 100 000-key sum 92.8 s); the edges were probed on refcore and the engine (`tmp/m1/g4/both.py`, `tmp/m1/g4/edges/`): `e1` 25 asks and `e2` 19 asks equal, byte for byte; `e3`'s two asks and one form of `e6` differ, each where the engine follows the spec (the STOP-REPORT below).

- **`prorata` (L §5.13; R-114).** `num/split.rs` (new, kernel) is the split, written once: `Weights::new` (each weight at least 0, the sum above 0, else `RUN-WEIGHTS`), `Weights::rounded` (`rest none`, each raw share rounded to the step by the mode), `Weights::last_rest` (`rest last`, the last key the remainder), `Weights::largest` (`rest largest`: a total off the step `RUN-PRORATA`; of the total's absolute value in steps, each key's floor, the leftover steps to the largest remainders, a negative total's shares the negatives of its absolute value's), `edge_tie` (the keys above the edge take a step, the keys at the edge all take one where they can, else the tie is held for the caller), `largest_first` and `merge` (the remainders sorted by a merge sort, since `Num::compare` can fail, so no sort comparator answers for a failed comparison), `Largest::shares` (the tie broken by the keys the caller's `ties by` chose, each checked to be a tied key, once; the shares' sum checked against the total, `ENGINE-INVARIANT` otherwise). `eval/prorata.rs` (new) reads it as refcore's `m_e_prorata`: the total (a hole is the share, `none` `RUN-NONE a value is none here: prorata`), the binding's members and weights (the holes of the domain, of a member whose `where` is one and of the weights, joined), key k (a key literal, `q1`, or a key in a year form, `2027` over `years`, resolved in the binding's domain as load resolves it; a key the binding leaves out has share 0, the split still made), the weights in the first's unit (`LOAD-UNIT prorata: weights of different units`), `RUN-WEIGHTS prorata weights [1, -1] are negative or sum to 0` (each weight as refcore's `fmt_q`, `1/3 (0.333333333333333)`), the step as `round` reads it (`minor(CUR)`, a figure, a read; a bare figure in the total's unit; `LOAD-UNIT … different units`, `RUN-DOMAIN prorata with a step 0.00 PLN that is not positive`), the mode (`eval/builtin.rs` `rounding_mode` takes the function's name now: `prorata: the mode is …`), and the share's type as load types it (money when the step is a whole number of minor units, under `rest last` only with a money total). The `ties by` (`Engine::break_tie`): e1 read for every tied key, a later term only among the keys the earlier left tied where that tie still decides which take a step (L §5.11 "e2 among those still tied"; G3's `tie_terms` and `pick_extreme`, now `pub(super)`); `RUN-TIE prorata has a tie at q1, q2, q3 that changes the result, and no ties by` and `… and its ties by doesn't break it`, naming every key the remainders tie, as refcore names them.
- **B1, a list index's lookup (pick P17; P §3.4, D2-5; #97's G7).** `model/index.rs`: an index's keys are private (`keys()`, `set_keys`, `add_keys`), and beside them `by_key`, their positions sorted by `lookup_order` (the kind of key, then its text, day, period or number; equal keys by position; read by nothing but the lookup, R-47), kept by every method that sets or adds keys, which also do `finish`'s text order for an open index (`finish` is gone); `pos` halves `by_key` and takes the first equal key, so an integer range's own halving is folded in. `load/indexes.rs` (built-ins, lists, ranges), `load/keys.rs` (an open index's key), `load/kinds.rs` (a version's keys) build through them; every other reader calls `keys()`. B1 on the debug binary: `b1_sum: 100 000` in 92.8 s before, 0.98–1.71 s after. The counters build's `--stats` on it is the same before and after, `ev=200001 def_hits=0 def_misses=0 reads=100000 distinct_reads=100000 num_results=100001`: **no counter sees a lookup's steps**, so B1's count row (the rules step's, P11) on today's counters would not catch a return to the walk; an instruction limit, or a counter in `pos`, would (for the coordinator).
- **Green ids:** `U:num/NUM-05` (`m1_checks_g4`, 1 of 1); `codes/m1` `rules/weights` (`RUN-WEIGHTS`) and `rules/prorata` (`RUN-PRORATA`), and the `rules` base's `q_split` answer; `carried/big/b1_sum` was byte-equal before and is now under 2 s on the debug binary (item 6's test waits on N7's `explain`, G12's). `RUN-PRORATA`'s second form (`<amount> arriving at or taken from <place> isn't a whole number of its step <u>`, L §3.6) has no vector (T's C4 didn't write it): it is a place's parts' split, not built in M1; `Weights::largest`'s `NotWhole` is the one it will use. `milestone-report: M1: 33/51 green` before, `M1: 36/51 green` after.
- **Files** (paths under `crates/tally/src/`), for the map (P19): new `num/split.rs` (kernel): the split of a total by weights to a step, `prorata`'s three rests, the largest remainder written once (R-114); new `eval/prorata.rs`: `prorata`'s reading, the total, the binding, the key, the step, the mode and the `ties by`, each share from `num/split.rs`. Changed: `model/index.rs`, `lib.rs`, `eval/{builtin, expr, extremes, arith, bindings, read, ty}.rs`, `load/{indexes, keys, kinds, across, check, cone, places, random, types}.rs`, and the crate's own `tests/{eval, load, read}_cases.rs` (`keys()`). No file split.
- **Size:** 23 files, +1 271 −59 in engine/crates (planned +260 −40, expected +420 −60): about 800 code lines, 200 doc lines and 190 test lines (`split.rs` 222 code, 85 doc, 121 tests; `prorata.rs` 495 code, 93 doc; `index.rs` +62 code, +22 doc, +70 tests). The plan sized `prorata` from refcore's 120 lines; the engine's reading of the step, the mode, the key and the share's type is twice that, and rustfmt lays the refusals out long.
- **Kernel functions touched:** `num/split.rs` (all new): `Weights::new`, `Weights::raw`, `Weights::rounded`, `Weights::last_rest`, `Weights::largest`, `edge_tie`, `largest_first`, `merge`, `Largest::tie`, `Largest::shares`, `SplitError::from`.
- **MB1, the mutation batch of G2–G4's kernel (`mutants-local --next -F 'checked_pow|bits|in_time|row_start|read_last|unreplaced|Weights::|Largest::|SplitError|edge_tie|largest_first|merge'` over `num/split.rs`, `num/rational.rs`, `read/gap.rs`, `read/rows.rs`; M1's 36 green tests as catchers; `hole/questions.rs` unchanged since M0; the walk's small `NotBuilt` arms left to MB3 and MB4):** 70 mutants in 32 min, 46 caught, 13 missed, 11 unviable, 0 timed out. Missed, equivalent (rows in `MUTANTS-SURVIVORS.md`, pick P37, from T's G1 notes): `rows.rs:363` `in_time`'s guard `span.is_always()` → `true` (load refuses every read with no law date of a dated row, `LOAD-LAWDATE`, T's `fixes/g1/u_nolaw`); `rows.rs:485` `>` → `>=` in `unreplaced` (two rows of one layer at the same keys known on one day are `LOAD-OVERLAP`, `u_same`; a candidate is never weighed against itself); `rows.rs:481` the first `||` → `&&` in `unreplaced` (a plain row stands beside a candidate only where the candidate replaces it; load refuses any other pair, `LOAD-OVERLAP … neither replacing the other`, probed in `tmp/m1/g4/p37/`; `u_wide` catches the second `||`); and `rational.rs:174` `<` → `<=` in `Num::checked_pow`, listed equivalent at G2's fix. Missed, for T, each told apart by a probe equal on both (`tmp/m1/g4/fort/t.tally`): `split.rs:105` `!=` → `==` in `Weights::last_rest` (a `rest last` vector: `prorata(10.00 PLN, 1 for j in ks, k, minor(PLN), half-up, rest last)` over three keys, `3.33 PLN`, `3.33 PLN`, `3.34 PLN`); `largest_first` → `Ok(vec![0])`, `Ok(vec![1])`, `:205` `>` → `==`, `>` → `<`; `merge` → `Ok(vec![0])`, `Ok(vec![1])`, `:229` `==` → `!=` (a `rest largest` vector with distinct remainders and steps left over: `prorata(10.00 PLN, w[j] for j in ks, k, 1 PLN, rest largest)` with weights 1, 2, 4, `1.00 PLN`, `3.00 PLN`, `6.00 PLN`, and −10.00 PLN, the negatives; T's `rules` family splits in quarters, whose remainders tie or are 0). And `split.rs:128` `||` → `&&` in `Weights::largest`, the guard of a step that isn't positive, which `prorata` refuses first: the guard is now one comparison (`step` compared with 0, not greater: `RUN-DOMAIN`), and a rerun on it (`--next -F 'Weights::largest'`, 49 s) caught its one mutant (`!=` → `==`, every step refused) and `:132`'s `delete !`; `Ok(Default::default())` unviable.
- **Follow-ups, not G4's:** a split memo across a def's cells (`prorata` keyed by k makes the split once per key, so n keys pay n splits; L §5.13 "O(n log n) once per (total, weights), memoised") → the performance milestone; `break_tie`'s `pick_extreme` scans the tied keys once per group taken (O(n · take) on a tie of n keys, as G3's `first_by_ties`) → the same; `prorata` over a tuple of bindings stops `NotBuilt` (load refuses it, `LOAD-PARSE`).

## STOP-REPORT 2026-11-08: refcore reads every term of prorata's ties by for every tied key and keeps the first hole; the engine reads a later term only where the tie still decides, and joins the holes

- **Kind:** other (refcore differs from the spec; the engine follows the spec)
- **Where:** refcore `eval2.py` `m_e_prorata` (for each tied key `vals = [self.ev(x, e2, ctx) for x in n.tie]`, then `for v in vals: if isinstance(v, Hole): return v`); the engine's `eval/prorata.rs` `Engine::break_tie`.
- **Rule or gate:** L §5.11 ("`ties by (e1, e2)` compares e1, then e2 among those still tied"), §8.1 ("It carries the questions of every operand, `where` and term it evaluated: the union, each once"), §5.13 step 2 ("keys whose remainders tie go by `ties by e`"), D-5; DONE gate 10. The class of #116 (P29; P35's B2, the argmin twin).
- **What happens:** `tmp/m1/g4/edges/e3.tally` (`index qs = [q1, q2, q3]`, 100.00 PLN in thirds by weight 1: one cent left, the three keys tied for it), `tally run e3.tally`: (a) `ties by (dp[j], hq[j])` with `dp` 1, 2, 3 and `hq` stated for q1 only: refcore `?"hq at q2: no value" (hq[q2])` at each key, exit 0; the engine `33.34 PLN`, `33.33 PLN`, `33.33 PLN` (dp alone gives q1 the cent; hq is read nowhere it decides). (b) `ties by hp[j]` with `hp` stated for q1 only: refcore `?"hp at q2: no value" (hp[q2])`; the engine `?"hp at q2: no value" (hp[q2]) ?"hp at q3: no value" (hp[q3])`.
- **Also met, load (G1's class, #113):** `prorata(amt, 1 for y in years, 2027, minor(PLN), rest largest ties by pos(y))` (`e6.tally`): refcore `LOAD-KEY the key of prorata is year4, not a key of years`; the engine reads 2027 as the year (L §2.1, picks P6 and P12, G1's year forms) and answers `33.33 PLN` (2026 takes the cent by `pos`).
- **What I tried:** nothing on refcore or the tests. The other 44 probe asks of `edges/e1` and `e2` (`rest none` and `rest last`, money and `num PLN` totals, a negative total, money and `%` weights, a key the `where` leaves out, a `where` that is a hole, `none` total and weight, `RUN-WEIGHTS` with fractional weights, `RUN-PRORATA` by a money step and a bare figure, `RUN-DOMAIN`, both `RUN-TIE` forms, two-term `ties by` where e2 decides or is a hole that decides, over a kind's things, an ask's literal steps) are byte-equal.

- **State:** 61d9763 carries the code; no test, spec or refcore file touched.

- 2026-11-08 G4's review (`tmp/m1/review-g4.md`: approve, 0 blocking): three of its non-blocking notes taken, 1, 2 and 4 (issue #108), on top of 98c4c91.
  - **NB2, the split made once per (total, weights) (L §5.13 Cost: "O(n log n) once per (total, weights), memoised").** New in `eval/prorata.rs`: `Splits`, a field of `Engine` (`eval/state.rs`). A `rest largest` split is kept under its total and step (each written as its reduced numerator and denominator) and its weights (compared whole). Its shares are kept too, one set for each choice of tied keys a `ties by` makes. The `ties by` is still read at every key: its terms are read in the members' scopes, so no kept split can answer for them. A refused split is not kept.
    - `rest none` and `rest last` keep no split. Each key's share already took constant time; the one exception is `rest last`'s last key, which sums the others once. The review's "`last_rest` O(n) per call" is that one key.
    - `weights_of` built the `RUN-WEIGHTS` text of every weight on every read. It now builds it only for the refusal (`weights_text`).
    - The binding's weights are still read at every key (the binding's cost, not the split's): `s[k] for k in qs` is n² weight reads, and the n log n split now happens once.
    - Debug binary, 800 keys (`tmp/m1/g4/nb/n800.tally`, `sum(s[k] for k in qs)`, user time): `rest largest` 4.22 s → 1.76 s, `rest last` 2.52 s → 1.77 s, `rest none` 2.50 s → 1.75 s.
  - **NB1, a share's conversion to money (R-76).** `share_value` refuses with `LOAD-UNIT … is not a whole number of minor units` only when the share isn't a whole number of minor units (`whole_minor`, refcore's `(q / CURRENCIES[cur]).denominator != 1`). Any other error from `num_money` keeps its own code. No probe reaches either: a share made with a step that is a whole number of minor units is itself a whole number of them.
  - **NB4, an open index's keys no longer re-sorted on every add (P §3.4).** In `model/index.rs`, `add_keys` on an open index whose keys are all its own names (every key that `load/keys.rs` brings) now inserts each key at its place in text order, found by halving, after any equal key, where the stable sort put it.
    - While every key is one of its own names, `pos`'s order is that same text order. The sorted positions are therefore 0..n, and the new one is pushed on the end, with no sort.
    - Any other key re-sorts the whole index, as before. `set_keys` now computes each key's text once (`sort_by_cached_key`).
    - An open index of 8 000 keys (`tmp/m1/g4/nb/o8000.tally`): 38.8 s → 34.4 s; of 2 000 keys: 2.49 s → 2.18 s. The remaining time is `load/overlap.rs` `check_overlap`: 3 635 of 3 701 samples (`sample`), a pairwise check over the rows. That is for the performance milestone, not the review's notes.

    - G4's own probes equal refcore, as before: `edges/` e1, e2, e4, e5; `fort/`; NUM-05; `q_split` on the rules base, on `rules/weights` and on `rules/prorata`. e3 and e6 still give the STOP-REPORT's answers.

  - **Kernel:** untouched (`num/split.rs` as at 3495fa8; MB1's result stands). New inline tests: in `prorata.rs`, a split kept once and its shares kept per choice; in `index.rs`, 500 open names added one at a time end up as one sort leaves them, and a foreign key falls back to the full sort.

Exception delta +0 (6; report-only hits 8, as locked: `Splits::made`'s first `entry(…).or_default()`, a `silent-default-call`, rewritten as a push or an insert). check full: "check full: green, 31 steps (582s)" (guard-test and rules-selftest skipped as their inputs are a green run's); `milestone-report: M1: 36/51 green`, unchanged.

## 2026-11-09 M1, G5: pins as a layer of the context, `with`, `as if`, `check` and `solve` in an ask (issue #108)

The builder of the M1 run, group G5 of `tmp/m1/build-plan.md` (pick P22, and the standing picks), on c415e32. Each red id was reproduced first on the debug binary: the four probes and `rules/solve` stopped `tally run: not implemented` (an ask's pins, an `as if`, `check`, `solve`). The edges were probed on refcore and the engine with G4's `tmp/m1/g4/both.py` (probes in `tmp/m1/g5/`): `deep.tally` (four `as if` nested, one of each kind: a value, `law`, `known`, `wording`; a check whose sides' law dates differ), `band-wording.tally` (five asks), `pertuple6.tally` (an expression pin) and `overlap.tally`'s nested pair are byte-equal. Three differ, each below: `pertuple5.tally` and `deep.tally`'s `payroll` (not built), `clock.tally` (P22), and `overlap.tally`'s `payroll` (the STOP-REPORT below).

- **The context's layer (E §3.2, §3.3; P §3.1, §3.5; D1-3).** `eval/context.rs` (new). `Ctx` gains `layer: Layer`. A layer is an interned `LayerData`, its pins and wording pins, compared as sets by target and value, never by their text, held in `Engine.layers` (the base, no pins, is slot 0).
  - A def's cell takes the layer trimmed to the pins whose items the def reaches on the graph (`trimmed`, cached per def and layer), so a def no pin reaches shares the unpinned cell. The memo's cells are keyed by the layer (`CellKey.layer`).
  - A layer is freed when the scope that opened it closes, and its cells with it (`close_layer`, `Cells::free`), unless a layer still open holds every pin of it; after a `Deep` unwind the layer is only left (`leave_layer`), since the replay needs its cells.
  - The timeline stays keyed by its cut. A pin on an item the moves reach (the cone of moves, places, prices, parts and fills), read where the item holding it reads the timeline, stops `NotBuilt` (`no_pinned_walk`); G6 keys the walk by (cut, world, pins).
- **Pins (L §5.11, §5.18 The world of an ask, §8.2).**
  - A value pin `x[k].f = v`: its keys made as a read makes them, `*` any key; the value in the target's type (`pin_typed`: a number pinned on money in its currency's minor units). A pin given by an expression is evaluated once, outside its `as if`; its reads go into the line's register, and its `pinned:` text is `x = <value>`. A later pin of the same target replaces an earlier one; a read takes the latest pin that holds at its keys.
  - `law D` and `known D` set the context's law date and cut. A date given by an expression prints `law e = D`; the days of one such pin formed at several keys merge into spans (refcore's `merge_pin_days`).
  - `wording D for [ids]`: the first pin naming an item holds (refcore's `wording_ok`). A row, a rule version or a band table that entered into force after D is not read: `read/rows.rs` `worded`, `enters`; `read/rules.rs` `rule_exists`; `read/bands.rs` `table_candidates`.
  - `with` on an ask: its pins are evaluated once, before the line; their `pinned:` entries print under every line; a key or date that is a hole makes the ask's line that hole; a `known` pin moves the cut the reads see (`ask_cut`).
  - Not built, each stopping `NotBuilt`: a scenario, sample or axis pin (G6, M3); a pin on a record field; a value pin formed per binding tuple, which L §8.2 prints once as written with its binding (`pinned: salary = gross[m] for m in months`, refcore's `_pertuple`; `tmp/m1/g5/pertuple5.tally`, and `deep.tally`'s `payroll` for an ask's own `for`; T's benchmark B-07x asks it). The text needs each binding's written form beside its variable in the scope.
- **`solve` (L §5.18 steps 1–3).** `eval/solve.rs` (new): F(v) = f − g on the grid lo + i·u. A grid is money in its currency, a number of a unit, an int or a rate of a period. F(lo) or F(hi) that is 0 is the answer; one sign at both ends is `RUN-SOLVE no sign change on [lo, hi]`. Otherwise it bisects exactly, mid = a + u·⌊(b − a)/(2u)⌋, until the bracket is one step wide, and returns the end nearer the root by the sign at a + u/2 (a tie goes up). Each step's pin of v is a layer of its own, freed after the step, and the register doesn't list it. A bracket off its grid is `LOAD-DOMAIN`; a rate's bracket and step of different periods is `LOAD-UNIT`; a `none` side is `RUN-NONE`; a hole F at a point evaluated is the result.
- **`check` (L §5.18).** `ask/check.rs` (new): `ok`; `differs: e = …, f = …` with `, difference d` for two numbers or two money values (`, difference refused: …` where the subtraction refuses); both sides where one is a hole, refused or the comparison is open. Where a side's own `as if {law D}` makes the sides' law dates differ, the register's last line is `law date L (left), R (right), cut K`. A check's difference of two keys stops `NotBuilt`: the key's index isn't kept at run.
- **`REQ-CLOCK` (E §11, P22, #112).** `ask/answer.rs`: an ask's `as known` after the model's `on` refuses the run, its one result, `refused: REQ-CLOCK as known <D> is after the model's on <on>`, exit 1; a request's refusal is never an ask's line. `tmp/m1/g5/clock.tally`: `refused: REQ-CLOCK as known 2026-06-01 is after the model's on 2026-03-01`; at `as known 2026-03-01` (`clock-on.tally`) the ask answers as before. refcore answers it (T holds `rules/clock` for #112).
- **N7 (the G4b review's: a table's slot per interned name), looked at.** The context uses no interner: pins, wording pins and the trim cache are keyed by the item's text, as the memo's cells are. A slot per `DefId` for the trim cache would be a fence when a budget row asks (P8).
- **Green ids (5 of the brief's 5 test ids, REQ-CLOCK built and held):** `U:law/LAW-03`, `U:surf/SURF-04/SURF-04`, `U:ask/ASK-04`, `U:val/VAL-04` (`m1_checks_g5`, 4 of 4); `codes/m1` `rules/solve` (`RUN-SOLVE`, `m1_code_run_solve`), the `rules` base's `q_solve` (`199.99 PLN`); `rules/clock` (`REQ-CLOCK`) built, held for #112. `milestone-report: M1: 36/51 green` before, `M1: 38/51 green` after. A partial answer (none written by T) and the "asks move no money" law (no `m1_properties` yet) are later.
- **Files** (paths under `crates/tally/src/`), for the map (P19): new `eval/context.rs`: the context's layer, the interned pin sets, value, date and wording pins, the trim per def, the freeing at a scope's end, the timeline's guard; new `eval/solve.rs`: `solve`'s exact bisection on its grid, a pin layer per step; new `ask/check.rs`: `check`'s line, its sides' texts and law dates. Changed: `ask/{answer, register}.rs`, `eval/{band, builtin, expr, memo, read, rules, stack, state}.rs`, `read/{bands, gap, rows, rules}.rs` (kernel), `lib.rs`, and the crate's own `tests/read_cases.rs` (`Clocks.wording`, `rule_in_force`'s arguments) and `tests/parse_cases.rs` (below). No file split.
- **Size:** 20 files, +1 970 −104 in engine/crates from c415e32 (planned +380 −90, expected +610 −145): about 3.2 times the expected size. The new files: `context.rs` 809 lines (156 of doc), `solve.rs` 408 (64), `check.rs` 200 (37); `register.rs` +141 (the `pinned:` merge and the check's law line); the wording pin through the readers and the kernel's reads, about +190. What drove it: the plan sized the context from refcore's `apply_pins_` and `pin_value` (about 120 lines). It didn't count the wording pin's reach into five kernel reads; `solve`'s grid of four kinds, each with its bracket and step checks; `check`'s texts for a hole, a refusal, a key outside its index and a window; or the layers' freeing and trimming, which refcore doesn't do (it keys every cell by the whole pin tuple).
- **Kernel functions touched:** `read/rows.rs` `worded`, `own_time`, `enters` (new) and `Clocks` (its field `wording`); `read/gap.rs` `read_input`, `read_last`, `read_reading`; `read/bands.rs` `table_candidates`; `read/rules.rs` `rule_exists`, `rule_in_force`.
- **MB2, G5's kernel (`mutants-local --next -F '(worded|own_time|enters|read_input|read_last|read_reading|table_candidates|rule_exists|rule_in_force)'` over `read/rows.rs`, `read/gap.rs`, `read/bands.rs`, `read/rules.rs`; M1's 38 green tests as catchers, at faf3703):** 43 mutants in 17 min, 29 caught, 7 missed, 7 unviable, 0 timed out.
  - Missed, for T, each told apart by a probe that is byte-equal on refcore and the engine. `bands.rs:67` `>` → `==`, `<`, `>=` in `table_candidates`, a band's table under a wording pin: `tmp/m1/g5/band-wording.tally`, a band in two versions of disjoint spans; `worded` (`wording 2026-06-30`, law 2026-08-01) is the hole `?"contribution: no value at 2026-08-01"`, and `worded_later` (`wording 2026-07-01`) is `400.00 DUK`. `rules.rs:43` `>` → `==`, `<`, `>=` in `rule_exists`, a rule version under a wording pin: `tmp/m1/g5/rule-wording.tally`, a `beats` rule whose version governs from 2026-07-01; `worded` is `1 000.00 DUK` (the base rule), `worded_later` and `worded_early` `1 100.00 DUK`. LAW-03's `june_unchanged` reads `ok` whichever version a mutant picks, so it catches neither.
  - Missed, listed since S: `bands.rs:76` `span.is_always()` → `true` (its row in `MUTANTS-SURVIVORS.md`, `R-87: missed: not built at S`, stands).
- **Exceptions:** +0 (6); report-only hits 8, as locked. faf3703 had ten more, each restructured away: five nested closures, two lifetimes (`SetPin`, the ask's `Form`: `form_line` takes the ask's body), an `entry(…).or_default()`, and a derived order on `Layer` (R-47: `Layer` has none now; the memo's cells, the freed set and the trim cache are keyed by its number).
- **Seen, not G5's (for T):** an ask's line that is a bare word no item names, `ask q: x`: refcore refuses the run, `LOAD-KEY ambiguous key x` (L §2.5; the class the 2026-11-05 M0 G8 entry names for a scenario's id, #75), and the engine prints `q: x`, with or without a pin (`tmp/m1/g5/m0plain.tally`, `m0pins.tally`). The crate's own `tests/parse_cases.rs` `the_forms_of_milestone_m0_parse` ran `ask q: x with law 2026-01-01` expecting the pins' `NotBuilt`; now that the pins run, that form's run is left out of its check, with this reason in its doc.
- **Check:** check full on the code of this commit: "check full: green, 31 steps (1402s)" (guard-test skipped as its inputs are a green run's); `milestone-report: M1: 38/51 green`. The run before it was red on two things, both fixed here: the lock (`derive-order-review` on `Layer`) and the crate's own `the_forms_of_milestone_m0_parse` (the pins form now runs).
- **Totals:** two commits, faf3703 (the code) and this one (the per-tuple guard, the tidy-up, the crate's test, this entry). Cost about 0.85 % of the week at faf3703 and 1.31 % at this entry (`engine/tools/usage`), inside the brief's 1.0–1.5 %.
- **Next:** G6 inherits `Ctx.layer` and `eval/context.rs` (`Layer`, `LayerData`, `Layers`, `Engine::pinned`, `as_if`, `open_layer`, `leave_layer`, `close_layer`, `trimmed`, `pin_of`, `wording_of`), where a world goes beside the pins in `LayerData`; `no_pinned_walk`, where the walk becomes one per (cut, world, pins); `Pinning`; the scenario pin's `NotBuilt` in `pinned`; `ask/check.rs`'s `Printed` for an ask's line; `Engine::with_pins`, `key_given`, `solve_line`; `register.rs`'s `check_register_lines`. ## STOP-REPORT 2026-11-09: two pins of different targets that hold at one cell: refcore takes the narrower key's, the engine the later pin's; the spec says neither

- **Kind:** the spec seems silent (refcore and the engine differ)
- **Where:** refcore `evaluate.py` `apply_pins_` (the context's pins sorted by `repr(q.key())`) and `pin_value` (the first pin in that order that holds); the engine's `eval/context.rs` `Engine::pin_of` (the latest pin applied that holds) and `LayerData::pin`.
- **Rule or gate:** L §5.11 (a pin's target and keys; `*` any key), §4.6 step 2 (the narrower row wins, by containment, for rows); DONE gate 10.
- **What happens:** `tmp/m1/g5/overlap.tally` (SURF-04's model; `gross` keyed by months), `python3 tmp/m1/g4/both.py . raise,payroll,return_check overlap.tally`: (a) `gross[2025-03] as if {gross[*] = 1000.00 DUK, gross[2025-03] = 2000.00 DUK}`: both `2 000.00 DUK`, `pinned: gross[2025-03] = 2 000.00 DUK`. (b) The same pins in the other order, `{gross[2025-03] = 2000.00 DUK, gross[*] = 1000.00 DUK}`: refcore `2 000.00 DUK` (its sort puts a stated key's text before `None`'s, so the narrower pin is first); the engine `1 000.00 DUK`, `pinned: gross[*] = 1 000.00 DUK` (the later pin). (c) Nested, the outer `{gross[*] = …}` and the inner `{gross[2025-03] = …}`: both `2 000.00 DUK`. The reverse nesting (outer narrower, inner `*`) differs as (b) does. Of two pins neither narrower than the other (`x[a, *]` and `x[*, b]`), refcore's choice is the order of the keys' text.
- **What I tried:** nothing on refcore or the tests; no T vector reaches it (every G5 id is green). The engine's reading is the one rule it has: a later pin replaces an earlier one of the same target, and a read takes the latest that holds.
- **What I ask:** a ruling, for the spec at M2's round. (Recommended) The narrower pin wins, as a row does (L §4.6 step 2), key position by key position; of two neither narrower, the inner or later pin. The engine changes `pin_of` to match, and refcore's `pin_value` is fixed at the round. Or: the later pin wins, as now, and refcore is fixed. T to hold `overlap.tally`'s `payroll` for the ruling.
- **State:** faf3703 carries the code; the next commit carries the tidy-up, the per-tuple guard and this entry. No test, spec or refcore file touched.

## 2026-11-09 M1, G6: worlds as a layer, asks in and across worlds, `diff`, E §9 item 3, expression rows (issue #108)

The builder of the M1 run, group G6 of `tmp/m1/build-plan.md`, on a547433. Each red id was reproduced first on the debug binary: the four carried `layers` asks and the LAW-13, SCEN-01 and SCEN-04 sets stopped `tally run: not implemented`; SURF-04's set was already byte-equal (its test red on the others). Edges were probed on refcore and the engine outside the tests (`tmp/m1/g6/pin/`, `inv3/`, `n11/`), each byte-equal unless said.

- **The world in the context (L §6.4; D1-3).** `model/world.rs` `World`: the layers of the reality's stack a read sees, `Lowest(d)` (0 is the model's) or `Whole`, never ordered by a derive (the memo orders it by `world_order`). `Ctx.world`; `Scenarios::world`, `sees`, `scenario_of`, `model_world`. A world under the top hides the sections of the scenarios over it, refcore's `in_world`: rows (`read/rows.rs` `candidates`, `index_had`, `kind_had`, `unstated_ok`), rule versions (`read/rules.rs` `rule_exists`), band tables (`read/bands.rs` `table_candidates`), index keys and things' entries (`model/reality.rs` `entry_seen`, `span_in`, `place_span`, `lifespan`; `eval/read.rs` `check_key`, `thing_seen`; `eval/bindings.rs`), moves (`walk/agenda.rs` `sorted_moves`, `walk/plan.rs` `keys_seen`), readings, observations and coverage (`book/reading.rs` `readings`, `book/observe.rs`). The memo's cell key holds the world, never trimmed: no cell is shared across worlds (P §3.2's sharing across worlds is a perf note for M9).
- **One timeline per (cut, world, pins) (E §3.3).** New `eval/timelines.rs`: each timeline's walk state (walks, plans, adjustments, projections, observations, links, booked amounts, signatures) is parked under its key, a world and the pins of the reader's layer that the walk reaches, and the one in force swapped in where a scope changes it (`open_layer`, `leave_layer`, the work stack's replay). G5's `no_pinned_walk` NotBuilt is lifted: `pin/m.tally` (a pin on the pay a salary move reads, by `with`, `as if`, and through a def) is byte-equal. A decision's running read that would change timeline stops NotBuilt. A freed layer's timelines are checked unmoved first (E §9 item 4). The booked amounts handed to the next ask (E §9 item 5) are the reality's own timeline's only.
- **Asks in a world (L §5.18, §6.4).** `ask/worlds.rs`: an ask is answered on the reality of the deepest world it names (its file's scenario, its `in`, a diff's worlds, its scenario pins, each `as if {scenario s}` its line or a def it reaches names, through load's `ask_worlds`), the one whose stack holds the others (refcore's `need`, `cands`, `ra`); worlds on two stacks stop NotBuilt (refcore's `cross_stack_diff`, `make_cross`). `ask/answer.rs`: the line's world is its `in`, else the model's; `law` and `as known` are evaluated in the model's; a scenario pin sets the world, `world: s`. `as if {scenario s}` evaluates inside in s's world and timeline.
- **`diff(e [for binds]) in s against b`.** New `ask/diff.rs`: e in each world, in its own timeline, keyed by the tuple `(v = x)` or the expression's text; `changed`, `added`, `removed` lines and the counts; the register of both worlds (refcore's `answer_diff`, `diff_combine`).
- **E §9 item 3 (`invariant.rs` `scenario_unchanged`, refcore's `m_inv3`).** Once a main walk in a scenario's world is done, every place's balance at the end of the day before `from` must be the world under it's, same cut and pins, else `ENGINE-INVARIANT scenario s: x holds … at the end of d, the base … (E §9 invariant 3)`; nothing is checked when a decision ran before `from`, a place with a hole, a refusal or no number is passed by, and so is a base walk that refuses. Run once from `walk/day.rs` `walk_through`. No input of T's or mine makes it refuse (load refuses a scenario row governing before `from`; the walk leaves out a scenario move's instance before it); `inv3/` walks the base silently. If the base's walk unwinds `Deep`, this walk's check is not run again (no answer changes; the check is lost).

## 2026-11-09 M1, G5's fix: the review's blocking findings 2–5, P38, P39, P41 (issue #108)

G5's builder, after G6's green commit and G4's fix (e2339f0), on the G5 review (`tmp/m1/review-g5.md`: blocking 5, B1 G4's) and picks P38–P42. An addendum to G5's entry above (2026-11-09 M1, G5). Every probe of the review (`tmp/m1/g5-review/p/`, `agent/`) and G5's own (`tmp/m1/g5/`) was run ask by ask on refcore and the debug binary (`tmp/m1/g5/fix/cmp.py`), before on e2339f0 and after on this commit (`before.txt`, `after.txt`, the differences in `before.diffs`, `after.diffs`).

- **B2, a deep unwind under a top-level `as if` never ended (`eval/context.rs`, `eval/stack.rs`, `eval/solve.rs`).** On a `Deep` unwind an `as if` left its layer, and the replay's inner `as if` then closed and freed every layer no open scope held: the outer layer's cells, which the replay and the read again needed. Now a scope a read unwound through ends with `end_layer`, which keeps its layer (`Layers.kept`) until the outermost read ends (`read_ended` in each of `stack.rs`'s six outermost reads, then `release_kept`); `free_layers` frees only what no open scope, no kept scope and no task on the work stack holds. `solve`'s step scope ends the same way. `context.rs`'s invariant ("never while a read that unwound past the counted depth still needs it") now holds. `agent/p1_33.tally` and `p1_40.tally` `deep1` (killed after 30 s before) and `p2.tally` `deep1` are refcore's (`deep1: 71`, `pinned: a = 5`, `pinned: b = 1`); `agent/p1.tally` `deep1` was `ENGINE-INVARIANT a read in a layer that was freed`, now `deep1: 205` as refcore.
- **B3 and B4, a step `to u` read at run (`eval/solve.rs` `step_number`, `step_in_grid`; refcore's `m_step_value`).** A step that isn't a number or money is `LOAD-UNIT the step of to is <v>` (`agent/p6.tally` s5, `none`: was `ENGINE-INVARIANT a solve's bracket load didn't type`); one that isn't positive is `RUN-DOMAIN to <v>: a step that is not positive` (L §7.1, §8.4: `agent/p1.tally` s3, `0.00 PLN`, was `RUN-DIV division by zero`; s2, `-0.01 PLN`, was the generic `RUN-DOMAIN` text); a unit that doesn't convert to v's is `LOAD-UNIT to <v>: the step and the value (<unit>) have different units`. All the review's s2, s3 and s5 asks (`p1`, `p1_20`, `p1_31`, `p1_33`, `p1_40`, `p2`, `p4`, `p5`, `p6`) are refcore's.
- **B5 and P40, a `solve` on one cell kept the ask's other pins of the item (`eval/context.rs` `solve_step`).** A step sets aside only a pin of its own cell (`Pinned::names_cell`); a `with w[*] = 3` holds at the item's other cells (L §5.18 "only that cell is pinned"). `agent/p1.tally` and `p6.tally` s1: `7` (was `8`, as refcore, which drops every pin of the item: #120, T holds it).
- **P38 (#119), the narrower pin wins (`eval/context.rs` `pin_of`, `Pinned::wider_than`; review NB4).** Of the pins that hold at a cell, the one that, at the first key position where two differ, names the key where the other has `*`: narrower-wins as for rows (L §4.6 step 2), and between two neither narrower the same choice refcore's sorted pins make, whatever the order the pins were written in. `g5/overlap.tally` `payroll` is refcore's now (`2 000.00 DUK`). G5's STOP-REPORT is ruled by it.
- **P39, a bare key literal on an ask's line (`load/check.rs` `unresolved_key`, `Reality.asks_unresolved`, `ask/answer.rs` `form_line`).** Load keeps, for an ask whose line holds a value that is a bare key literal no position resolves (`x`, `(x)`, a key of one index or of two), `LOAD-KEY ambiguous key <k>` at the ask's line; the run meets it where the line is evaluated, so it refuses the run in the base's world and the ask alone in a scenario's, and an ask not run never meets it (refcore's `e_name`, its `keylit` arm). `g5/m0plain.tally`, `m0pins.tally` and `g5/fix/keylit2.tally`'s five asks are refcore's (`refused: LOAD-KEY ambiguous key x (m0plain.tally:6)`).
- **P41 (#112), `with known D` after `on` (`ask/answer.rs` `before_clock`).** The cut the ask's `with` pins give, past the model's `on`, refuses the request as `as known` does: `refused: REQ-CLOCK with known 2026-06-01 is after the model's on 2026-03-01` (`agent/p6.tally` c2; refcore answers `3`, as it does `as known`'s c1, both held for #112). The line's words are mine, beside P22's; the round states them in E §11.
- **NB1, a value pin formed per binding tuple: left, for G14** (the budget; `p/pins.tally` a5, `agent/p4.tally` pk1–pk3 stop `NotBuilt` as before).
- **Probes, before → after (the same 294 asks of 22 files; `keylit2.tally`'s six asks are new):** before 251 byte-equal, 40 differ, 3 past 30 s; after 267 byte-equal, 27 differ, none past 30 s. 23 differences and the 3 timeouts became byte-equal (B2, B3, B4, P38, P39). 10 asks left refcore by ruling: the nine `s1` (P40, #120) and `c2` (P41, #112). The 27 that differ: those 10; `c1` and the two `q_due` (`p/clock`, `g5/clock`; P22, #112); the per-tuple pins of NB1 (`p/pins.tally` a5, `agent/p4.tally` pk1–pk3, `g5/pertuple5.tally`, `g5/deep.tally` `payroll`); a decision's `q_first`, not built, G10's (`p/base`, `p/clock`, `p/solve`, `g5/clock`, `g5/clock-on`); a `sum` over a range of money, not built (`g5/pertuple`, `pertuple2`, `pertuple3`).

## 2026-11-09 M1, G5's second fix: the fix review's F1, its non-blocking 1 and 3 (issue #108)

G5's builder, after G6's fix (f8635f5), on the review of G5's and G4's fixes (`tmp/m1/review-g45-fix.md`: one blocking, F1, from 6de2b7d). A second addendum to G5's entry above (2026-11-09 M1, G5), the last fix turn for G5. Every probe of G5's review (`tmp/m1/g5-review/p/`, `agent/`), of the fix review (`tmp/m1/g45-fix/`, its `k/` single-ask files and the two-file `sc/`) and G5's own (`tmp/m1/g5/`) was run ask by ask on refcore and the debug binary (`tmp/m1/g5/fix/cmp.py`; `sc/` by `tmp/m1/g4/both.py`), before on f8635f5 and after on this commit (`tmp/m1/g5/fix2/before.txt`, `after.txt`, the differences in `before.diffs`, `after.diffs`).

- **F1, an ask's `with` layer freed before the ask opened it (`eval/context.rs` `release_kept`, `eval/stack.rs` `read_ended`).** 6de2b7d's end of an outermost read let go of the kept layers and swept every layer no scope held; `with_pins` hands back a layer it has just made, which no scope holds until the ask opens it, so a `with` pin whose value unwound through an `as if` freed its own layer (`g45-fix/min.tally` `ask z1: d with a = r[40]`: `ENGINE-INVARIANT`, at `ask/answer.rs`'s read in it). Now the read's end only lets go of the kept layers; they are freed at the next scope's end that no scope holds them past (`close_layer`), by which time a layer a read handed back is open. `z1: 162` / `pinned: a = 81` / `pinned: b = 1`, as refcore; `memo2.tally` z1, z3, z5 too.
- **Non-blocking 3, a failing replay left the kept layers kept (`eval/stack.rs` `replay`, `replay_tasks`).** The replay's refusal ends the read: `replay` lets go of the kept layers, then returns it.
- **Non-blocking 1, P39 inside an `if` and a list (`load/check.rs` `unresolved_key`, `Reality.asks_unresolved`, `ask/answer.rs` `unresolved_word`).** Load keeps the ask's line for a line value typed a key literal or a list (a list literal types as a list whatever its items); the run, after the line is evaluated, refuses `LOAD-KEY ambiguous key <k>` at that line on the first value that is an unresolved key literal or a list holding one, the literal `if` took or the list's first (refcore's `e_name`, its `keylit` arm, met where evaluated): an `if` with a hole condition stays its hole, and the line's earlier values are read first, as refcore. `g45-fix/key.tally` a4 (`if w[q1] == 1 then q1 else q2`) and a19 (`[q1, q2]`), and `k/a4`, `k/a19`, are refcore's (`refused: LOAD-KEY ambiguous key q1 (a4.tally:23)`).
