## STOP-REPORT 2026-11-10: E §10's "Long derivations fold" gives no length or layout; refcore prints every step

- **Kind:** the spec seems unclear (silent on the form)
- **Where:** `engine/spec/ENGINE.md` §10, "Long derivations fold. A recurrence over 300 months prints its first and last steps and a count."; refcore `ask.py` `explain_node` (every node printed); the engine's `ask/explain.rs` `derivation`.
- **Rule or gate:** E §10; DONE rule 3.
- **What happens:** `tmp/m1/g11/ai2/m.tally` `ask e_acc: explain acc[40]`, a 40-step recurrence: refcore prints all 40 cells nested, `[1]` to `[40]`, and so does the engine, byte for byte. Neither folds. The spec doesn't say from what length a derivation folds (300 steps? any recurrence?), how many first and last steps print, or the count line's words, depth and numbering.
- **What I tried:** nothing past refcore's print; any fold would be a layout of my own.
- **What I ask:** (Recommended) refcore's full print stands for M1, and the next spec round writes the fold: its length, its first and last steps, the count line's text (for instance `… 294 more steps of acc …` at the folded step's depth), and a vector. Or a ruling now, with that text.
- **What I did not do:** invent a fold; touch a vector.
- **State:** 666cfdc, green; this report is committed with G11's entry.

## STOP-REPORT 2026-11-10: `readers-of` an event kind: E §10 lists the bindings over it, refcore prints `nothing reads k`

- **Kind:** the spec and refcore disagree, and the spec doesn't say how a binding prints
- **Where:** E §10, "`readers-of x` … Of an event kind, every binding over it or over a kind it is a subkind of"; refcore `check.py` `Checker.readers_of` (graph nodes only; a kind is none); the engine's `ask/explain.rs` `node_of`.
- **Rule or gate:** E §10, L §5.18; DONE rule 3.
- **What happens:** `tmp/m1/g11/ro/m.tally`: `kind fees: transfers`, `ask nfa: count(f in fees over 2026)`, `move pay: fees`, `ask r_fees: readers-of fees`. Refcore prints `r_fees: nothing reads fees`. The engine stops the run `not implemented` (exit 69). By the spec, nfa's binding `f in fees over 2026` reads fees, and so would a binding over `transfers`.
- **What I tried:** the spec's set is clear; its print isn't. A binding sits inside an item, so the line could name the item (`nfa`) or the binding. It is also unclear whether `move pay: fees`, which declares an instance of the kind, reads it, and where those readers' own readers come.
- **What I ask:** (Recommended) the line lists the ids of the items that hold a binding over k or over a kind k is a subkind of, as the first layer, then their readers as for any node, each layer by id; a move of kind k is no reader (its kind is its type, not a read). Refcore is fixed at the round; T holds an event-kind vector for the ruling.
- **What I did not do:** print refcore's `nothing reads`, which the spec contradicts; guess a binding's print.
- **State:** 666cfdc, green; this report is committed with G11's entry.

## STOP-REPORT 2026-11-10: `explain` of a def whose cause is a balance: refcore prints the walk's own reads, E §10 a balance in its own form

- **Kind:** the spec and refcore disagree (for G12's form)
- **Where:** E §10, the rows "a def cell … under the winner its causes" and "a balance | its last reading … or the opening, and every movement since, up to that day"; refcore `evaluate.py` (a walk built while tracing is traced like any read); the engine's `eval/trace.rs` `tracing` (no node while a shared structure evaluates, its reads its own, E §3.2) and `ask/explain.rs` `traced`.
- **Rule or gate:** E §10, E §3.2; DONE rule 3.
- **What happens:** `tmp/m1/g11/ro/m4.tally`, `def b: money PLN = bal(konto, d + 1 day)` and `move pay` (`on d`, amount `fee[q1]`), `ask e_b: explain b`. Refcore prints `[1] b = 490.00 PLN`, `rule base: won`, `[2] row card_2026:d …`, `(card_2026:d: see [2])` and `[3] fee[q1] = 10.00 PLN` with its rows. Those are the movement's day and amount, read by the walk, shown as b's causes, with no balance line. Had another read of the ask built the walk first, refcore would print none of them, since its walk cache isn't the memo it swaps. The engine stops the line `not implemented` (exit 69); without that stop it would print `[2]` alone, the cause dropped, since the walk's reads are its own.
- **What I ask:** (Recommended) the cause prints in E §10's balance form under the def: the last reading or the opening and the movements since, grouped by day, each with its status and id. G12 builds it; refcore is fixed at the round, and T holds such vectors "for #n" meanwhile.
- **What I did not do:** trace inside the walk; print a derivation without its cause.
- **State:** 666cfdc, green; this report is committed with G11's entry.

## 2026-11-10 M1, G12: `explain` of a balance, a projection, a `low` and a movement instance; `readers-of` an event kind (issue #108)

The builder of the M1 run, group G12 of `tmp/m1/build-plan.md`, on c82ef44. Each red id was reproduced first on the debug binary. The forms were probed on refcore and the engine outside the tests (`tmp/m1/g12/`, copies of the fixtures), byte for byte with the exit code, unless said.

- **A balance** (E §10, L §5.18; `ask/walked.rs`, new): `explain bal(x, d)`, `bal(x, d start)`, `bal(x, u, d)`, `with uncertain`.

  - **`from:`** the last reading, with its word, row id, value, instant, version, source and locator, and an anchor's or a pad's gap role and gap. Without one, the opening: `0 at the start of its lifespan, D`, or `no reading or opening before it`.
  - **Then the movements since, by day:** each `id[.leg] (status): ±amount`, or a hole, a refusal or a reading. An uncertain instance's legs show where the clause counts them.
  - **No body** for a refused value, for `bal(x)` outside a decision, or for a hole day. A refusal met in the body prints as `refused: …`.
- **A projection** (`bal(x, d) from t`, `low(…) from t`), from the plain twin's walk, never F5's tree (`walk/projection.rs` `projection_walk`, the walk a read builds). It prints:
  - `holder: ask <id> (an ask line leaves out nothing, L §5.14.1)`;
  - `clauses: windows none, random none, with|without uncertain`;
  - `start:`, the state at the start of t;
  - `counted:`, by day;
  - `left out: none`;
  - for a `low`, also `lowest: Q at the end of D`.

  From before S: no lines, but the main walk is built, and its warnings print as refcore's do.
- **A `low` without `from`** (L §5.18): `lowest end-of-day balance of P on A .. B: V, on D1[ (through D2)]`, then D1's balance form.
- **P63 (#132), the balance cause:** a `bal` among a def's causes prints as the node `[n] bal(place, day[ start])[ with uncertain] = v`. The balance form follows under it, indented, from the walk the read used (`eval/trace.rs` `Kind::Balance`, `trace_balance`; `walk/reads.rs` `trace_bal`). The indent and the node's label are my own design: the ruling gives the form, and refcore prints the walk's own reads there. For T and the next round.
- **P63's other half, `readers-of` an event kind:** the first layer is the items that hold a binding over k, or over a kind k is a subkind of (`Graph.bound_kinds`, noted at load in `load/binds.rs`). Their readers follow as for any node, each layer by id. A move of kind k is no reader.

  - **A plan:** `plan, day D, booking cut C, status S`.
  - **A decision:** `decision, day D, status S`, then `stratum N, walk point (D, stratum s, m|nnnnnn)`, or `stratum N, not run`. An unordered cycle's run is `first|nnnnnn|m`. Then `read at its walk point:`, with every place it read there.

  - **A move read by its name** (L §2.5; refcore's `e_moveref`, `find_inst`; `eval/expr.rs` `move_ref`): the instance the cut sees at those keys, else a movement known by its id alone. Before, a bare name read as a word, and keys stopped `not implemented`.

### Addendum 2026-11-10: G11 review's B1, B2 and B3 (`tmp/m1/review-g11.md`; ruling P64, #133)

- **B1, an extreme's winner past the counted depth.** An extreme explains its first winning term by reading it again alone (`trace_winner`), once `trace_drop` has forgotten the cells the terms traced. That read evaluates the recurrence anew, a cell deeper than the term's own read, so it can pass depth 32 and unwind. The nodes it made on the way kept no value and stayed in the trace's cell map. The memo already held those cells, so the read after the replay referenced the value-less nodes: `= ?`, and a chain cut short. Now `def_cell` calls `eval/trace.rs` `trace_cell` in place of `trace_value`; it forgets a cell whose node ends on a stop that is no refusal, so the read after the replay evaluates it again under the trace. Each replay leaves the cells it computed in the trace, so the chain the re-read walks shortens until it fits. `agent/deep_max` (34 steps), `deep_agg` (the aggregate form), `deep_max300` (300 steps) and `d1` `e_hi2` (80 steps) now print refcore's bytes.
- **B2 and B3, each item once (P64).** `ask/explain.rs` `readers` now walks items, not graph nodes. `item_of` takes a move's day node (`MoveOn`) to its move, and a scenario's expression row (`Srow`) to the input or def that reads it. `nodes_of` gives an item's nodes back, so its readers are those of all its nodes. Each item prints once, in the first layer that reaches one of its nodes, by id. Results: `p/c` and `c2` `r_d2: pay2`; `p/b` `r_d2: b2, bo, lastpx, pay2, elp, ib2, ibo, ilp, v`; `ro/m` and `m2` `r_d: b, pay, nfa, v`; `agent/r1` `r_d: b, pay, v`; `agent/s1` `r_a2: b, c, c_sc`. Refcore prints `pay2, pay2`, `…, ilp, pay2, v`, `b, pay, pay, v, nfa`, `b, pay, pay, v` and `c, input, b, c_sc` there, so T holds those seven for #133.
- **NB1, left for the round.** The item graph has no edge from a balance read to the moves that book into it, and refcore agrees. A static inverse would make every balance reader a reader of every move, which is wider than `inputs-of`'s traced list. Not cheap, and the review calls it a spec question.
- **NB8, no change: not a defect.** A label is one rule in force at a cell's law date. `in_force` keeps at most one version per label (L §5.11 step 2), and two rules of one label, neither a `replace`, are refused at load (`LOAD-LABEL`). Refcore keys its states by label too.
- **NB10, left for the round**, as the brief says.
- **Probes.** `tmp/m1/g11/tools/sweep.sh` runs each ask against refcore (365 asks):
  - the review's `p/` (`a`, `b`, `c`, `c2`, `d`, `e`, `pr/sign`) and `agent/` (`ax`, `b1`, `d1`–`d5`, `deep_agg`, `deep_max`, `deep_max300`, `ip`, `mv1`, `mv2`, `mvs`, `mx`, `o1`, `o2`, `r1`, `s1/`);
  - mine in `g11/`: `v/` (the five ids' files), `ai`, `ai2`, `st`, `ro`, `g7`, `un`, `a13`.
  - The full bytes are in `before.txt` and `after.txt`, one line per ask in `before-sum.txt` and `after-sum.txt`.
  - Before (c4c2bfb): 338 SAME, 27 DIFF. After: 337 SAME, 28 DIFF.
  - 12 asks changed: B1's 5 went DIFF → SAME; B2/B3's 6 went SAME → DIFF, and `s1` `r_a2` stayed DIFF with new bytes, all by P64.
  - The other 21 DIFFs are byte-for-byte as before: not built (`agent/b1` (bands over dates), `mv2`, `mvs`, `p/b` `enn`, `ai2` `e_pick_a`, `un/t`, `un/w`); the review's item 13 (`mx`, a tie broken by a hole); and G12's open `ro/m` `r_fees` and `ro/m4` `e_b`.
  - After the restructure for check full's report-only hit, the 61 `readers-of` asks were re-run, byte-equal to `after.txt`.
- **For T:** the B1 vectors are refcore-equal (`deep_max`, `deep_agg`, `deep_max300`). The P64 vectors are against the ruling, since refcore is wrong there: a move whose day and amount read x (`p/c2`), a reader through the day (`ro/m`), a scenario row (`agent/s1`).
- **Mutants:** not run this turn, to stay within two heavy jobs. The kernel functions are `trace_cell`, `readers`, `item_of` and `nodes_of`.
- **Files:** `eval/trace.rs`, `eval/rules.rs`, `ask/explain.rs`. **Exceptions:** +0. The first check full was red on one new report-only hit (a named lifetime on a list helper), which was restructured away.

## STOP-REPORT 2026-11-10: an integer key at a range stepped by a fraction: L §2.5 takes it, refcore refuses it

- **Kind:** the spec and refcore disagree
- **Where:** L §2.5 (at a stepped range's position a key is a value of the range's type, one of a + i·s ≤ b for a whole i ≥ 0); refcore `evaluate.py` `range_values` (a value's `isint` is `a.isint and step.denominator == 1`) and `vkey`, which keys a value by its `isint` too; the engine's `eval/ranges.rs` `range_holds`, `value_key`.
- **Rule or gate:** L §2.5, §5.6; DONE rule 3.
- **What happens:** `tmp/m1/g13/pz/p3.tally`, `input by_frac[f in 0.5 .. 2.5 step 0.5]: num` with rows `by_frac[1.5] = 0.15`, `by_frac[2.0] = 0.2`. `ask a_frac_missing: by_frac[2]`: refcore `refused: LOAD-KEY 2 is not a value of 0.5 .. 2.5 step 0.5` (exit 1); the engine `a_frac_missing: 0.2`. With `at_int = 2`, `by_frac[at_int]`: refcore `RUN-KEY 2 is not a value of …`, the engine `0.2`. `by_frac[1]`: refcore `LOAD-KEY 1 is not a value of …`, the engine the hole `?"by_frac at 1: no value"` (1 is 0.5 + 1·0.5, a value with no row).
- **What I tried:** the spec's test is numeric: 2 = 0.5 + 3·0.5 is one of the range's values, and an int is a number (L §3.2). Refcore's refusal comes from its value carrying a flag the spec doesn't name: 2 the integer and 2.0 the range's value are two keys to it.
- **What I ask:** (Recommended) the spec stands: a key at a stepped range is tested by its number, so an int key of a fractional range is the value it equals. Refcore is fixed at the round; T holds the three forms "for #n".
- **What I did not do:** refuse an int key by its class, which no section says.
- **State:** 4e593bf, green; this report is committed with G13's entry.

## STOP-REPORT 2026-11-10: a day over a period, and a rate times money, met at run: the spec refuses them, refcore's run computes them

- **Kind:** the spec and refcore disagree
- **Where:** L §3.2 Period units ("`day` is a dimension of its own, so `1 day / 1 year` is refused (`LOAD-UNIT`)"); L §3.3 ("Anything else (`rate × money`, `rate + num`) is refused at load"); L §8.4 What isn't defined is refused ("at load where the types show it … and otherwise at the key (`RUN-DOMAIN`)"); refcore `check.py` `_arith` (a `dyn` operand, a leg of unit `any`, is checked "as it is computed") and `evaluate.py` `binop`, which then computes it; the engine's `eval/arith.rs` `arith` (the day-over-period stop), `rate_mul` and `add_sub` (rate arithmetic outside L §3.3).
- **Rule or gate:** L §3.2, §3.3, §8.4; DONE rule 3.
- **What happens:** `tmp/m1/g13/sites/pz3/` (`gen3.py`, `y1.tally`, `y5.tally`): `kind evs: event certain` with `leg what: any`, `move mv: evs` with `leg what from konto to world amount fee` (10.00 PLN). `sum(m.what.amount * 1 day / 1 year for m in evs over d0..d0)`: refcore `10 PLN*day/year`, `… / 1 day * 1 year` `10 PLN*year/day`, `… * 1 day / 1 month` `10 PLN*day/month`; with `input rr: rate` (5 %/year), `m.what.amount * rr` `0.5 PLN/year`, `rr / m.what.amount` `0.005 1/PLN/year`, `m.what.amount * rr * 1 month` `1/24 (0.0416666666666667) PLN`, `rr * m.what.amount / m.what.amount` `0.05 1/year` (all exit 0). The engine stops each `not implemented` (exit 69). Every other use of such a leg (a test, a date argument, a key, a comparison, a field: 18 probes, `gen4.py`) is refused at load by both, byte-equal.
- **What I tried:** the spec refuses both operations by their units; load can't see a leg of unit `any`, so §8.4 puts the refusal at the key. The code is either `RUN-DOMAIN` (§8.4) or `LOAD-UNIT` met at run, as G4a's N6 made `a rate times a duration of another period dimension`. The text is unwritten.
- **What I ask:** (Recommended) refused at the key: `LOAD-UNIT` with refcore's load texts (`… day is a dimension of its own (1 day / 1 year is refused, L §3.2)`, `rate × money` as `rates of different periods`' sibling), met at run as N6's are; refcore's run checks a `dyn` product's units at the round. Until then the two sites stay "not implemented".
- **What I did not do:** compute the product (the spec refuses it); choose the code or the text.
- **State:** 4e593bf, green; this report is committed with G13's entry.
